Can I sign up for only a business profile? I don’t want a personal Venmo

A business profile on Venmo requires an accompanying personal account.  

You must either sign up for a personal account and business profile at the same time or set up your personal account alone first. Business profiles are available to Venmo users with existing personal accounts.  

 

Where can I sign up?

If you don’t already have a Venmo account, you can sign up for a business profile from venmo.com or the mobile app.

You also must be in the United States and have a mobile device capable of accepting SMS messaging. If you do already have a Venmo account, you’ll see the option to create a business profile in the app. Check out the drop-down at the top of the Me tab and select Create a business profile to get started.  

  

Why does it say my phone number or email is already on file?

It’s possible that you may have already created a Venmo profile and simply need to sign in. If that is the case, you can use your email, phone number, or username and password to log into your account.   

If you previously created a personal Venmo account, you should sign in to that account and create your business profile there. Business profiles are available to existing Venmo users who have a personal account.  

If you’ve created a personal account and a business profile, you’ll sign in with your personal account credentials. Once you’ve signed in, you can toggle between your personal account and business profile.  

Still not able to log in?  Learn more about logging in.   

 

Do I need to provide an EIN for my business?

We’ll only ask you to provide an EIN if you use one. If your business has an EIN, you’ll be able to submit your EIN for tax reporting purposes during signup. 

If your business does not have an EIN, you can create a business profile as a sole proprietor, using your personal information and SSN or ITIN instead.

For more information about tax reporting on Venmo.

 

Can I create a business profile for an association or club?

Business profiles can be created to represent a wide range of associations and clubs. When creating your business profile, you can choose from the following categories under Association:

  • Automobile Association 
  • Civic, Social, and Fraternal Organization 
  • Membership Club (Sports, Recreation, Athletic), Country Club, and Private Golf 
  • Membership Organization 
  • Political Organization 
  • Schools and Educational Services 

It’s worth noting that all business profiles are subject to fees and tax reporting requirements.

Marketing Your Business on Venmo

What does it mean to market my business on Venmo?

Venmo’s business profile settings are designed to manage your marketing preferences. It will help spread the word about your business to the Venmo community. Depending on your preferences in your business profile Settings, we’ll market you in Venmo feeds, searches, and emails.

Venmo wants to help you maximize your presence with existing and potential customers. These features are designed to let people know who you are, and where your business excels. You can toggle this functionality on or off from your Settings menu.

 

What if I have privacy concerns?

As a business on Venmo, you’ll have full control over your marketing settings. You can disable any or all of these five features individually.

Venmo is constantly trying to improve the experience of our users, including business profile account holders. These features are designed to draw attention from existing and potential customers, and ultimately increase your sales.

If you’d rather disable these features, you can toggle them off after selecting Market your business on Venmo from the Settings menu of your business profile.

 

Are these marketing features available for personal accounts?

At this time, marketing features are only available to business profiles and charity profiles.

See this article for more information on how to create a business profile.

 

What do these marketing features do? 

There are five features that can be selected independently: 

  • Search: Turn this on if you want your business to appear in search results on Venmo. If you’re the top business result for a customer’s query, we’ll include additional details like photos you’ve uploaded and the names of any friends who’ve paid you.
  • Feed:  Enable this for payments sent to your business to appear in the feed  
  • Email:  Venmo can send emails and notifications to people showcasing your business profile information 
  • Use business address for marketing: Toggle this on to display your business address in search results, emails, and in Venmo users’ feeds  
  • Market beyond physical location: If your business isn’t limited by your geographic location (e.g., you ship goods to your customers) 

 

What is this email I received?

Venmo sent this email to inform you that your business profile is eligible to be featured in the feed. We’re providing you with an opportunity to check your business profile settings to ensure you approve of being featured in our feed.

If this is not something you are interested in, please head to your marketing settings, and turn off the toggles for both Feed and Use address for marketing.

 

What is the benefit of being featured in the feed?

Being featured is a terrific way to set your business profile apart from other businesses and sellers on Venmo. You can use featured business posts to show local Venmo Users your products or services in the feed. This feature will give you the opportunity to be discovered by local buyers that may not know about your business.

 

Can I post on my own feature or edit a feature that has been shared?

Venmo wants to make sure that featured posts are as informative as possible, so they stand out from payments in the feed and spread the word about your goods or service to potential buyers.

Currently, we do not have a way to create or edit a business profile post. Featured business posts are created from your business profile, which can include your marketing settings as well as your profile and background photo.

 

I do not want to be featured in the feed. How do I turn it off?

If you prefer to not be featured in the feed, you can opt-out by logging into your business profile and selecting “Marketing your business on Venmo” from the Settings menu. To completely opt out of being featured, you will need to turn off the toggles for both Feed and Use address for marketing.

 

How does Venmo create the post?

Venmo wants to make sure that business feature posts contain as much information as possible and can stand out from transactions in the feed.

Business profile feature posts are generated using both profile and background photos, creating the images you see on the feature. We’ll use your selected location and marketing settings to share these features with local Venmo users, so potential buyers can discover you.

Buying & Selling on Venmo

Buying and Selling on Venmo FAQ

Your personal Venmo profile can be used to send and receive payments for good and services. Learn all about buying and selling (without a business).

If you have a Venmo business profile or are interested in signing up for one, please view our Business Profile Help Center. 

 

 How does buying and selling work on Venmo? 

When you pay another personal profile on Venmo, you may have the opportunity to tag that payment as a purchase. Eligible payments will be covered by the Venmo Purchase Protection Program if this option is selected by the sender, and Venmo will charge a small fee to the payment recipient.

Users who receive payments tagged as purchases will incur a fee. This fee amount is automatically removed from the total amount received.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

Venmo’s Purchase Protection Program is designed to help both buyers and sellers manage their next steps when things don’t work out with an eligible purchase.

  • If you’re the buyer, and what you bought isn’t delivered, arrives broken, or is just plain wrong, let us know. Venmo Purchase Protection may be able to help make it right.
  • If you’re the seller, Venmo Purchase Protection can help prevent you from losing money due to unauthorized transactions or claims that the buyer never received their purchase.

 

What’s eligible for Venmo’s Purchase Protection program?  The terms and conditions for Venmo’s Purchase Protection program are available in the User Agreement. 

Some examples of situations that could be covered by Venmo’s Purchase Protection program include:

  • If you bought a book, but received a DVD
  • If you purchased an item described as authentic, but received a knockoff
  • If the item you bought is missing major parts that the seller didn’t report
  • If you purchased three items but only received two
  • If the item was damaged during shipping

This payment wasn’t for a purchase. What do I do?

If you accidentally tagged a payment you sent as a purchase, or if someone accidentally tagged a payment they sent to you, please contact our Support team. 

There isn’t a way to un-tag or recategorize a payment after the sender identifies it as a purchase, but we may be able to return the fee for payments tagged in error. Alternatively, our Support team may be able to assist in returning the payment to the sender so they can pay you the correct way. It’s important to understand the potential tax implications of receiving payments tagged as purchases, especially if the amount received meets or exceeds the reporting threshold. Please visit our Tax Center for more information.

Disputing these transactions will not rectify this situation, but if there an issue, please contact us as soon as possible 

  

Why isn’t the ability to tag a payment as a purchase showing in my app?   If you don’t see the ability to tag a payment as a purchase, it means the option isn’t available for that particular payment. You may have this option for other payments in the future, but please note that not all payment activity is eligible for Purchase Protection.

You can review the terms and conditions of the Venmo Purchase Protection Program here.

If it’s a time-sensitive payment, you could try making this transaction on PayPal instead.  

Learn more about opening a dispute. 

Goods and Services, Marked as purchase, sale.

Buying from Personal Profiles

If you’re tagging a payment to a personal profile as a purchase, read on. If you are buying from a business profile, please view our Business Profile Help Center. 

How do I tag my payment as a purchase?

When you initiate a payment to another personal profile on Venmo, you may see the option to tag the payment as a purchase.

After tapping or clicking on Pay/Request, select your recipient(s) and enter the amount & note, then tap Pay. On the next screen, there may be a toggle shown below your payment method. You can tap the toggle to let us know that you’re making a purchase, and your payment will be covered by the Venmo Purchase Protection Program if eligible.

 

What does it mean to tag my payment as a purchase?

When you tag a payment as a purchase your payment will be covered by the Venmo Purchase Protection, if eligible, and the seller (recipient) will pay an associated fee. Eligibility criteria for the Venmo Purchase Program can be found in the User Agreement.

If there’s an issue with the purchase, like if you receive something that’s different from what you bought, an item was damaged in shipping, parts are missing, or your purchase never arrives, please contact us as soon as possible. We’ll investigate any eligible purchases that did not go as planned, and you may receive a refund if the investigation is decided in your favor. 

 

Can I tag a request as a purchase?

When you fulfill a payment request, you can tag it as a purchase. Remember, only the party sending the actual payment can choose to identify it as a purchase.

 

Is the seller notified when I tag a payment as a purchase?

If you tag a payment to another user’s personal profile as a purchase, we’ll let the recipient (seller) know about the fee.  

 

I tagged a payment to another user’s personal profile as a purchase and the seller didn’t fulfill their end of the agreement. Can I dispute the payment?

If you’re having an issue with a payment you tagged as a purchase, we encourage you to work directly with the recipient (seller). For situations where you didn’t receive what you paid for or a service was not rendered, you have the option to dispute the payment in your Venmo app. Filing a dispute will open a formal investigation so Venmo can help mediate the issue for eligible purchases.

To open a dispute in your Venmo app, follow these steps:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Select the transaction you want to dispute
  3. Tap Need Help?
  4. Select the statement that best describes your situation
  5. To include other transactions, select Add more
  6. After selecting the transactions you want to dispute, tap Add
  7. Tap Next
  8. Add any additional information
  9. Tap Submit IssuePlease note: Failing to provide certain information may delay the resolution of your case. If you are having trouble disputing your transaction, please contact our Support team.

Can I use Venmo to buy or sell merchandise, goods, or services?

Can I use Venmo to sell goods or services?

The only way to accept payments for goods and services on Venmo is to be explicitly authorized to accept Venmo for purchases. This can be achieved by applying for a business profile or  by having the sender tag a payment to a personal profile as a purchase.

How can I use Venmo to purchase goods or services? 

As a customer or “buyer”, you may use Venmo to pay for goods or services by paying an authorized business profile or by tagging a payment to a personal profile as a purchase (when it’s available).

You can also use your Venmo account to pay for goods and services by using a Venmo Debit Card at merchants that accept the card, through mobile websites or apps that are approved to offer Venmo as a payment option, or by using your in-store QR code at select merchants.

For more information about accepting payments for your business in the Venmo app, you can read more about business profiles or learn about making payments for goods and services to personal profiles.

Are there any rules about buying and selling on Venmo? 

Outside of the scenarios mentioned here, Venmo may NOT otherwise be used to receive business, commercial, or merchant transactions, meaning you CANNOT use Venmo to accept payment from (or send payment to) another user for a goods or services, unless explicitly authorized by Venmo.  

If something goes wrong with a payment sent to a personal profile and that payment was not identified by the sender as a purchase, please contact our Support team as soon as possible. You may also consider working with local law enforcement, depending on the situation.

  • Please note that we may be unable to recover funds or merchandise lost as a result of non-approved purchase activity

Additional information about Venmo’s Purchase Protection Program can be found in the User Agreement.

Can I use Venmo to collect donations for my non-profit or otherwise?

Venmo offers charity profiles for charitable organizations (and 501c3s) that have confirmed their charity status on PayPal. If you have a PayPal charity account, you can connect to Venmo from the PayPal App Center. Learn more about signing up for a Venmo charity profile.

You may also be able to work with PayPal or Braintree to integrate Venmo as a checkout option in your organization’s app or website. You can find more information about accepting Venmo for your business here.

Personal profiles should not be used to receive funds for user-created donation campaigns or non-profits.

For Merchants

What is Venmo?

Venmo provides an easy way for friends and family to pay each other and bypass the need for cash or checks on the go. Beyond payments to friends and family, Venmo can be used to purchase items directly from participating approved apps and online stores, and at in-store merchants with certain Point of Sale (POS) systems.

For more information, check out this article.

 

I’m interested in accepting Venmo. How do I get this set up?

Please see the Venmo website for more information about accepting online or in-app payments and how to get started:  

You have a few options if you want to accept Venmo for payments to your business: 

 

  • Accept Venmo through PayPal’s online checkout process, where users can select Venmo as a payment option. Please keep in mind that this option will not add a separate Venmo payment button on your website. (This option is automatically available to PayPal merchants. You can find more information here.)
  • Create a charity profile for a charitable organization if you have confirmed your charity status on PayPal. With a PayPal charity account, you can connect to Venmo from the PayPal App Center. Learn more about signing up for a Venmo charity profile.
  • Accept Venmo QR code payments from your customers with in-store QR codes. If you’re interested in this option, please reach out to your Point of Sale (POS) provider. (Venmo does not provide support for individual merchants’ POS systems.)

Venmo should not otherwise be used to complete purchases unless explicitly authorized by Venmo.

Payment Holds for Businesses and Sellers

This article includes information about tax holds and non-tax-related payment holds. Continue reading for details about payment holds or click here to jump to Tax Holds.

 

Payment Holds

What are payment holds?

Payment holds are a common industry practice that Venmo implements to help ensure a safer environment for both buyers and sellers on our platform. 

As selling products and services online carries some inherent financial risk, these holds provide an easy path to resolution in the event of disputes on eligible payments. For business owners and sellers, this practice also makes it easier to return payments when there’s a need to refund a customer.

When a payment is on hold (not for tax-related reasons), businesses and sellers can expect funds to be available in their Venmo account (minus the business transaction fee or seller transaction fee) within 21 days.

 

What causes a payment to be on hold?

There are a few different reasons why your payment may be on hold. Here are a few common causes:

  • You’re a new seller with Venmo or you’re an established seller but have opened a new account 

  • The payment you received may be considered unusual for your selling pattern

  • Your account was inactive for a while

  • You’re selling a product or service that can potentially cause dissatisfaction with customers

  • Multiple customers reported problems or requested refunds 

 

How will I know if my payment is on hold?

If you receive a payment that’s been placed on hold, you’ll be notified in the confirmation email for the payment. Payments that are on hold are also displayed with a small “On Hold” badge on your Activity tab (or under “Transactions” on your personal profile’s Me tab), and you’ll see a held amount at the top of that page. 

 

My payment is on hold. What should I do?

When your payment is on hold, the money still belongs to you—it’s just temporarily on hold. Held payments are automatically released within 21 days, meaning that you’ll have access to the funds from any held payment once the hold period expires. When this happens, you’ll receive a notification in the Venmo app and via email explaining that your funds are available.

You can select any payment from your personal transactions feed under the “Me” tab to see whether it’s on hold and view additional payment details. For business profiles, check the Activity tab. This information is also available when viewing your statement from the web.

 

How can I avoid payment holds?

Selling products and services online carries some inherent financial risk. To account for this and to create a safer environment for buyers and sellers on Venmo, payment holds are automatically applied to eligible payments. Since these holds are automated, there isn’t a way to stop them from happening. 

 

 

Tax Holds

What are tax holds?

Due to IRS reporting thresholds, you will need to provide Venmo with your tax information if you’re receiving payments for sales of goods and services. To avoid possible tax holds and backup withholding on your received payments, you can provide your tax information in the Venmo app. This does not apply to payments between friends and family (not tagged as purchases).

To help ensure compliance with federal regulations, payments you receive for sales of goods and services in excess of the reporting threshold will be placed on hold if we don’t have your tax information. You will have an initial grace period to provide your tax ID after meeting the threshold, after which point your held payments will be subject to 24% backup withholding. You will continue to experience holds and monthly backup withholding until you provide your tax information.

If you haven’t received any payments for sales of goods and services, this won’t affect you. If you do receive these payments, we’ll let you know once you’re approaching the reporting threshold and whether any of your payments are on hold.

Learn more about current tax reporting requirements here.

 

I received a notification asking me to provide my taxpayer information. What should I do?

If you received this notification, it means that the total value of payments you’ve received for sales of goods and services on Venmo meets or exceeds IRS reporting thresholds for this kind of income. 

You can provide your tax ID in your Venmo app – just follow the prompt from your Notifications page or look for Identity Verification under your Settings menu.

  • If you already have payments on hold, you should also see a notification on the Me tab or your business profile home screen in the Venmo app
  • Your “Payment details” pages will also have a button so you can “Provide tax info” if needed 

Providing your tax ID ahead of time can help you avoid tax holds and backup withholding, so you can access your money faster. 

Learn more about identity verification on Venmo.

You can also watch the short video below for more details about this requirement and how to add your info.

 

Why is Venmo sending my money to the IRS? I didn’t send these payments.

If you see outgoing payments to the IRS from your Venmo account, those payments account for backup withholding due on the payments you received. You can tap on any of these outgoing payments to view additional details, including a link to the original payment you received.

Remember: You can provide your tax ID in the Venmo app to avoid backup withholding on your payments. 

 

What is backup withholding? Why is this happening?

Backup withholding is when a portion of your payments is sent to the IRS to ensure that any tax due on those payments is paid. Without your tax ID, we’re required to send 24% of each payment you receive for goods & services to the IRS as backup withholding to cover any potential income tax due.

Remember: You can provide your tax ID in the Venmo app to avoid backup withholding on your payments.

Learn more about backup withholding on the IRS website. 

 

I already provided my taxpayer info. Why is my payment on hold?

If you’ve already provided your taxpayer information, you may still experience payment holds for a few different reasons. Read more about payment holds and what can cause them.

Selling with Personal Profiles

If you’re receiving a payment for a good or service to your personal Venmo profile, read on. If you have a business profile, please view our Business Profile Help Center.  

 

Why am I charged a fee when my buyer tags a payment as a purchase?

The seller fee for payments tagged as purchases enables Venmo to provide the Venmo Purchase Protection Program for eligible payments. Venmo’s Purchase Protection program can help prevent you from losing money due to unauthorized transactions or claims of missing merchandise.

Learn more about buying and selling on Venmo

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

This payment wasn’t for a purchase. Can the fee be refunded?

If someone sent you a payment tagged as a purchase and you didn’t sell them anything, please have the sender contact our support team to refund the fee.

It’s important to understand the potential tax implications of receiving payments tagged as purchases, especially if the amount received meets or exceeds the reporting threshold. You can visit our Tax Center for more information.

For unexpected payments from users you don’t know personally, please contact our support team as soon as possible. Do not attempt to return payments from strangers on your own. Read more about common scams on Venmo.

 

I got a notification that someone sent me money for a purchase. What is this? 

If the sender tags a payment as a purchase when sending money to your personal profile, we’ll notify you that they did so. This means that the sender let us know they’re paying you for an item or service, and the payment (if eligible) will be covered under Venmo’s Purchase Protection Program.

 

I sent someone a request but received less money than I expected when it was fulfilled

It’s likely that the sender tagged the payment as a purchase.

If someone sent you a payment tagged as a purchase and you didn’t sell them anything, please have the sender contact our support team to request a refund of the fee. 

 

I’m refunding my buyer. Is the fee refunded as well? 

For payments intentionally tagged as purchases by the sender, Venmo will not return the fee amount if you need to issue a refund. This means that you (as the seller) will need to cover the fee amount when refunding your buyer to ensure that they receive the full amount of their payment back.

You can refund a buyer by making a new payment to them. Please note that this “refund” won’t be tied to the original payment you received. Instead, you’ll see this as a new payment in your Venmo transaction history. 

 

Does Venmo have any other features for sellers?

If you frequently use Venmo to sell things, we recommend exploring business profiles. Some benefits of creating a business profile include, but are not limited to:  

  • Increased visibility for your business in the Venmo app  
  • Ability to generate referrals among socially engaged Venmo users  
  • Options to provide potential customers with more information about your business in the app, including links to a website and social media accounts  
  • In-app photo gallery to feature products and services (only available for some industries)  
  • Ability to refund customers directly from the Venmo app  
  • Formal tax documentation for business profiles that meet or exceed federal and state reporting thresholds  
    • This information is not intended to be and should not be construed as tax advice. You should consult a tax professional regarding your reporting of these transactions.
  • Ability to enable tipping on payments your business receives  
  • QR Kits for easy, touch-free payment options 
  • Ability to sign-up to accept contactless payments from compatible cards and digital wallets with Tap to Pay
    • Learn more about Tap to Pay on iPhone
    • Learn more about Tap to Pay on Android

Charity Profile Settings & Features

Charity Profile Account Settings

Charity profiles have unique features and settings. Learn how they differ from your personal profile. 

 

How can I make changes to my charity profile? 

Charity profiles can be managed entirely from your Venmo app. After you’ve signed in and toggled to your charity profile, tap the gear at the top of your screen to access your Settings. 

  • To make changes to public, donor-facing details on your charity profile, tap Edit from the Profile tab
  • Tap Payment Methods to connect a bank account 
  • Tap Face ID & Passcode (iOS) or Passcode code & biometric unlock (Android) to enable Passcodes for your profiles 
  • Tap Privacy to make changes to the privacy settings for donations you receive 

Note: We cannot edit this information after your charity profile is created (This information will be private and is not shared with your customers):   

  • Legal charity name   
  • Employer Identification Number (EIN)   
  • Registration type   
  • Charity phone number    
  • Charity contact address  
  • Business type (registered business)  
  • Registered business type (non-profit, corporation, or partnership)  

If you need to make any changes to your charity name or category, you’ll need to contact Support.  

How can I spread the word about my charity on Venmo? 

Tap the Share button at the bottom of your charity profile to share your profile via text or email, or download your QR code image. 

Want more ways to share? Check out Marketing your business on Venmo from the Settings menu to make changes to your marketing preferences. Here’s a breakdown of what these preferences do: 

  • Search – Allow donors to find your charity in search results on Venmo 
  • Feed – Enable Venmo to highlight your charity in the feed with enhanced posts highlighting your profile 
  • Email – Enable Venmo to highlight your charity in Marketing emails sent to other Venmo users 
  • Use business address for marketing – Enable Venmo to use your charity’s business address for location-specific marketing 
  • Market beyond physical location – Expand your reach by enabling Venmo to include your charity in marketing materials not specific to your location  

Donors can also share posts in the Venmo feed after donating to your charity. When shared, these posts will feature your charity’s profile and background photos, along with a Donate button so their friends can easily begin a donation without having to search for your profile. 

How can I view my charity profile? 

Tap the eye icon at the top of the Profile tab for a preview of what donors see when they visit your profile. 

Can I use my charity profile to send payments or donations to other Venmo profiles? 

Charity profiles are designed to receive donations only. If you need to make an outgoing payment to another profile on Venmo, you can use the toggle at the top of your Profile tab to switch to your personal profile.

Charity Profile Donation Fees

Fees for charity profiles allow Venmo to provide this service for verified charities. Read on to learn more about how these fees work.

 

How do fees work for Venmo charity profiles?

Venmo charity profile owners are charged a low fee for every donation received of $1.00 or more.

This fee is a standard rate of 1.9%+$0.10 for each donation.

For example, if a donor sends $100.00 to a charity profile, $2.00 of that payment would be charged as a donation fee and the charity profile would receive $98.00.

Venmo is not able to refund this fee. Fees like this help Venmo to continue to provide this service and develop the service further.

 

Why doesn’t the donor have to pay the fee?

The fees for using a Venmo charity profile are charged to the charity rather than the donor.

It’s worth noting that Venmo users who send donations to a charity profile using a credit card will be exempt from the standard 3% Venmo fee on payments funded by credit cards.

Learn more about the standard Venmo fees that will apply to you and your donors.

 

Can I get a refund for the fee?

The fees assessed to donations received by charity profiles allow Venmo to continue providing and improving the service. We are not able to refund these fees.

Charity Profile FAQ

Create a unique Venmo profile for your charity and gain access to millions of Venmo users who can donate and learn about your charity all from their Venmo app.

 

Who can create a charity profile? 

Venmo charity profiles are available for charitable organizations and 501(c)(3)s. To create a Venmo charity profile, you’ll need to start out with our parent company, PayPal. If you don’t have a PayPal business account yet, click here to get started on PayPal.

If you already have a PayPal business account, there are just a few requirements to open a Venmo charity profile:

  1. The owner of your PayPal charity account must be the same person who will own the Venmo account
  2. Your charity status must be confirmed on PayPal

Learn about other requirements for using Venmo.

 

What are the benefits of creating a Venmo charity profile? 

Once your charity status is confirmed on PayPal, you’ll gain the ability to create a charity profile on Venmo and collect donations from an engaged community of donors on our platform.

Here’s what you can expect from your Venmo charity profile:

  • Easy setup with important account details imported from your linked PayPal charity
  • Blue checkmark badge displaying your verified status to Venmo donors
  • Increased visibility on Venmo with enhanced and shareable posts from donors 
  • Low transaction fees of 1.9% + $0.10 per donation received 
  • Donation history and account statements, complete with donor contact information  
  • Same-day access to funds from donations (fees apply) 
  • Separate wallet for your personal payments and transactions on Venmo 
  • Photo gallery to help tell your charity’s story 
  • QR code and QR Kits for collecting touch-free donations in person 

How can I create a charity profile? 

Your Venmo charity profile starts on PayPal. If your charity isn’t on PayPal yet, click here to start creating your PayPal business account.

Make sure your charity status is confirmed on PayPal, then go to the Business Tools page, find Venmo, and select Get Started.

First, we’ll bring you over to Venmo to sign up for a new account or sign in if you already have a Venmo account.  

  • If you’re creating a new account, you’ll need to enter and verify your mobile phone number by following the prompts on your screen. Then, choose an email address and password for your account and agree to Venmo’s terms and conditions. 

Please note, the legal first and last name on your Venmo account must match the name of your PayPal business account owner.

Next, we’ll gather your information from PayPal to start creating your Venmo charity profile. Here’s what you’ll need to do: 

  1. Enter a username, description, category, and keywords to help donors find your charity profile 
  2. Enter your contact information and provide any social media or website links you wish to include on your profile 
  3. Preview your charity profile and tap Publish 

What info is being shared between PayPal and Venmo for my charity profile?

By creating a Venmo charity profile and connecting your PayPal account to Venmo, you give us permission to collect information about you and your charity.

  • Personal information: Includes your Full name, Email, Account verification status, and PayPal ID

 

  • Charity information: Includes your Business Address, Business Category, Business Email, Business Name, Business Owners, Business Registration Information, Business Type, Business Subcategory, and Tax Identifier

Learn more about PayPal’s Privacy Policy.

Learn more about Venmo’s Privacy Policy.

 

I don’t have a PayPal account. Can I still create a Venmo charity profile?

No. You’ll need a PayPal Business account in order to set up a charity profile on Venmo. After creating your Business account with PayPal, you can confirm your charity status and gain the ability to create a Venmo charity profile. 

Learn more about getting started with a PayPal Business account for your charity. 

 

How can I convert my Venmo business profile to a charity profile? 

There isn’t a way to convert a Venmo business profile into a charity profile or to use your contact information on more than one Venmo account. You can create a charity profile with a new phone number and email address or contact us for help closing your current account so you can start over. 

 

Can Venmo charity profiles be managed on the web? 

No. There isn’t a way to manage your charity profile via the web. However, you can access your charity profile transaction history and statements from the web. Here’s how: 

  1. Log into your Venmo account from a web browser 
  2. Click Statement from the sidebar 
  3. Select your charity profile from the dropdown at the top of your screen 

Donors will still be able to donate to your charity using a web browser.

Charity Profile Featured Posts

Featured posts can highlight your charity profile to a new audience of donors, even if they haven’t donated to your cause yet. Learn more here!

 

Why did I receive an email about having my charity featured?

If your charity profile is eligible to be featured in the feed on Venmo, we’ll send you an email before publishing your post. You can use this opportunity to decide whether you want to be featured and check your charity profile settings in the Venmo app. You’ll have 5 days to make changes if you don’t want to be featured.

If this isn’t something you’re interested in, you can select Marketing settings from your Settings menu and turn off the toggle for the Feed.

 

What is the benefit of being featured in the feed?

Having your charity featured in the feed can be a great way to spread the word about what you do or who your charity benefits. Typically, your profile will only be highlighted to friends of previous donors if they choose to share their activity.

Featured posts aren’t tied to donations; they’re a separate opportunity to have your profile stand out to new potential donors on Venmo. If you’re eligible to be featured, Venmo can highlight your charity name and photos you’ve uploaded to help tell your story and drive donations from engaged Venmo users. Featured charity profile posts also include a donate button to make new donations seamless – score!

 

How does Venmo create featured posts?

Featured posts are designed to stand out from other transactions in the feed, and they’re meant to provide potential donors with the essential details of your charity at a glance.

To generate featured posts, we’ll use your charity’s profile photo and background photo. Venmo users who see your post can tap on it to view your charity profile and learn more, or they can tap the Donate button direct from the post itself.

 

Can I post my own feature or edit a published featured post?

Currently, there isn’t a way to create or edit your own featured post. It’s worth noting that featured posts will include your charity’s profile and background photos, so you can update those images if you’d like to highlight something else on your post.

Learn more about making changes to your charity profile.

 

I don’t want my charity profile to be featured in the feed

If you’d prefer to not have your charity profile featured in the feed, you can opt out by updating your marketing settings in the Venmo app:

  1. From your charity profile home screen, tap the Settings gear
  2. Tap Marketing settings
  3. Turn off the toggle for Feed

If you change your mind about being featured in the future, you’ll need to turn the Feed toggle back on.

Charity Profile Sign Up Errors

Using your charity profile should be as seamless as possible. If you’ve received an error, read on to learn what you can do to fix it. 

 

“There’s an issue with your charity account status” 

We received a notification from PayPal about the status of your charity account. You’ll need to sign in to your PayPal charity account in order to address this issue. In the meantime, your Venmo charity profile will be unable to receive donations. 

“We’re unable to publish your profile” or “Let’s try that again” 

These indicate a timeout or connection error. Try again or cancel and exit to start over. 

 

“We can’t confirm your info” 

We couldn’t match your information to the owner of your PayPal charity account. If you aren’t the owner of the PayPal charity account and already have a Venmo account with your name, you’ll need to sign in to a different account or create a new account on Venmo.  

 

“This link isn’t shareable” 

The link you used to get to this page isn’t a valid entry point. To set up your Venmo charity profile, you’ll need to get started from your PayPal charity account homepage. 

 

“Looks like you’re already on Venmo” 

You already have a Venmo account associated with the phone number you’re using. Sign into your existing account or use a different phone number to create a new account for your charity profile.

Marketing Your Charity on Venmo

Want to get your charity in front of more Venmo users? Learn about your charity profile marketing preferences.

 

What does it mean to market my business on Venmo?

Managing your marketing preferences can help spread the word about your charity to the Venmo community. Depending on your preferences (selected in your Settings), Venmo may market your charity profile in Venmo feeds, searches, and emails.

Venmo wants to help you maximize your presence to existing and potential donors. These features are designed to let people know who you are, and what your charity does.

To make changes to your charity profile marketing preferences, follow these steps in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to your charity profile (you may need to switch over from your personal profile first)
  2. On the Profile tab, tap the Settings gear
  3. Tap Market your business on Venmo

What do these marketing features do? 

There are five marketing features that can be selected independently: 

  • Search: Turn this on if you want your charity to appear in search results on Venmo. If you’re the top charity result for a donor’s query, we’ll include additional details like photos you’ve uploaded and the names of any friends who’ve paid you.
  • Feed: Enable this for donations sent to your charity profile to appear in the feed  
  • Email:  Venmo can send emails and notifications to people showcasing your charity profile information 
  • Use business address for marketing: Toggle this on to display your charity’s business address in search results, emails, and in Venmo users’ feeds  
  • Market beyond physical location: If your charity isn’t limited by your geographic location

 

What if I have privacy concerns?

As a verified charity on Venmo, you’ll have full control over your marketing settings. You can disable any or all of these five features individually.

These features are designed to draw attention from existing and potential donors, and ultimately increase the volume of donations you can collect on Venmo.

If you’d rather disable these features, you can toggle them off after selecting Market your business on Venmo from your charity profile Settings menu in the Venmo app.

 

Are these marketing features available for personal accounts?

At this time, marketing features are only available to business profiles and charity profiles.

QR Codes for Charity Profiles

QR codes are a quick and easy way to send and receive money on Venmo. Learn more about using them for your Venmo charity profile.

 

How can I share my charity profile’s QR code? 

From your charity profile (in the Venmo app), tap QR Code. When you open the scanner and tap Venmo Me, you’ll see your charity profile’s QR code along with your profile picture. Here you’ll have options to share your charity profile’s code via text message, email, and more.  

 

How do I print my charity profile’s QR Code? 

You can print a high-quality PDF copy of your charity profile’s QR code from the Venmo app:

  1. Tap QR Code
  2. Open the scanner and tap Venmo Me (you’ll see your charity profile’s QR code along with your profile picture)
  3. Tap the printer icon to get started printing your QR code

 

How do donors use my charity profile’s QR code?

Donors can use their Venmo app to scan your QR code and go to your charity profile. From there, they can tap Donate to start a donation.

It’s also worth noting that donations you receive can be shared on Venmo depending on the privacy settings for your charity profile. Payments shared publicly are shown on your charity profile and in the Venmo feed, lending you some extra exposure to your donors’ communities.

Read more about adjusting your privacy settings.

Receiving Donations FAQ

Verified charities can collect donations from Venmo users. Read on to learn about receiving donations to your Venmo charity profile.

Want to get started collecting donations on Venmo? Learn more about creating a Venmo charity profile.

 

Are there any fees for collecting donations on Venmo?

Venmo charity profiles will incur a low fee of 1.9% + $0.10 per donation. Learn more about fees for charity profiles.

 

How can donors find my Venmo charity profile?

For the fastest and easiest way to get donors to your charity profile, we recommend sharing your QR code. Just tap QR Code from your charity profile home screen to open your QR code.

Aside from scanning your QR code, donors can find your profile by searching for your charity name. 

Donors who send public donations on Venmo will also have their donations visible in the feed for their network to see. Donations shared in the feed include your charity profile’s background photo and a direct link to donate to your charity as well.

Donors can also discover your profile when browsing available charities in their Venmo app. Donors can get to this page by tapping the search bar on the Home tab, then Make a donation. Here, they can search for charities by name or browse by category.

 

Are there any limits on the number of donations charities can receive?

There are no limits on the number or monetary amount of donations a Venmo charity profile can receive.

 

Are there any bank transfer limits for charities?

Charity profiles do have a weekly rolling limit for bank transfers. This limit has two parts:

  • $100,000 weekly rolling limit on standard transfers to a bank account
  • $50,000 weekly rolling limit on instant transfers to a bank account or debit card

Each bank transfer you make will count towards this limit for 7 calendar days. 

Remember that payment and bank transfer limits for your charity profile are separate from your personal profile.

Learn more about transfer limits for your personal profile.

 

How can I add a bank account to my charity profile?

To add a new bank account to your charity profile, you can follow these steps in the Venmo app (you may need to toggle to your charity profile first):

  1. Go to the Profile tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear
  3. Tap Payment Methods
  4. Tap Add bank or card…

From there, you’ll be able to add a bank account.

If you add a bank account, you can do so using your bank credentials or by manually entering your account number and routing number. If you use your account number and routing number, please do not enter the check number. Venmo will also send microtransfers to verify the account information, so please make sure you have at least $2 in your bank account to avoid potential overdraft fees.

 

Does Venmo send tax donation receipts to donors?

For each donation your charity profile receives, Venmo automatically sends an official donation receipt on your behalf to the email address associated with the donor’s Venmo account.

The donation receipt is included as part of the donor’s payment confirmation notification and can’t be turned off.

If needed, you can download a statement to view your donation history and donor details. Learn more about accessing your Venmo transaction history.

In accordance with our User Agreement, we ask that donor email addresses only be used to acknowledge the donation and for tax receipt purposes, not for general marketing.

 

My donor is having trouble completing a donation

If donors are unable to donate to your charity profile, we first recommend checking your Venmo notifications to ensure that your charity profile is in good standing.

If you haven’t received any correspondence from us regarding the status of your charity profile, it’s possible that your donor’s connection timed out. If the issue persists after they try again, have your donor contact our Support team so we can take a closer look. 

 

I need help refunding a donation

You’ll need to have enough funds in your charity profile’s Venmo balance in order to cover a refund, including the donation fee. If your balance can cover the full amount you want to refund, please contact our Support team with the following details:

  • Date of the donation in question
  • Donation amount
  • Sender’s Venmo username

Cryptocurrency

Buying Cryptocurrency on Venmo

Are you looking to buy crypto on Venmo? Check out this page for some helpful info.

 

How do I buy crypto in the Venmo app?

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Select your preferred cryptocurrency - we’ll update the displayed current price every few seconds 
  3. Enter the dollar amount for your purchase and hit Review 
  4. Your app will show you a breakdown of how much of the cryptocurrency you’re about to buy, along with any fees 
  5. Tap Buy to complete the purchase 
    • Remember, crypto purchases are final and cannot be reversed 

You can use your Venmo balance, your bank account, or your debit card to buy crypto on Venmo. At this time, you cannot use a credit card or your Venmo Credit Card to make these purchases.  

 

What limits exist for buying crypto on Venmo?

You are limited to $20,000 in crypto purchases per week. There is also a limit of $50,000 in crypto purchases in a 12-month period.  

 

Why is there an exchange rate for cryptocurrency?

Since crypto is digital money, you’re essentially swapping one currency for another when you buy and sell it. The exchange rate tells you how much crypto or dollars you’ll get in the swap and refreshes often because the value of crypto is constantly changing. 

 

Are there fees when buying cryptocurrency on Venmo?

You will incur a fee when you buy or sell most cryptocurrencies on Venmo. The fee will vary based on the purchase or sale amount. Venmo displays that amount when you are making a transaction. You can learn more about how these fees are calculated in Venmo’s Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

If you use a linked bank account or debit card for a Venmo transaction, your bank may charge its own fees, like overdraft fees or insufficient fund fees. Venmo is not responsible for fees charged by your bank.

There are no fees to buy or sell PYUSD on Venmo.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

Will my crypto be bought instantly every time?

It’s possible for your purchase of crypto to take longer to process and it may not always be successful. If the purchase fails, we’ll notify you that the purchase was not successful.

Crypto Price Alerts

Do you want to keep an eye on how crypto prices are changing? Venmo’s Crypto Price Alerts can help. 

 

How do crypto price alerts work?

When you turn on crypto price alerts, we’ll send you push notifications when the price of your selected crypto goes up or down by your chosen percentage in a day.

Alerts are designed to keep you informed about movements in the crypto market, but should not be your sole way of monitoring and making decisions involving crypto. Crypto price alerts may be delayed or disrupted (including due to market conditions and data outages), and you should verify any information you receive from a price alert before you act on it. Price alerts are provided for informational purposes only, and do not constitute a recommendation by Venmo to buy, sell or hold any asset. 

Push notifications for Venmo must be turned on in your phone settings in order to receive push notification price alerts.

You can learn more about how crypto price alerts work in our user agreement.  

 

How do I set up price alerts? 

You can sign up for and manage your price alerts in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Tap the relevant cryptocurrency
  3. Use the Price Alerts toggle to turn on price alerts

You will specify your selected percentage for price changes when you turn the feature on. This selection can be modified at any point.

If you’ve already verified your identity with Venmo and set up your Venmo account for buying/selling/holding crypto, you can also sign-up for price alerts when you are buying crypto. 

 

How many alerts will I receive? 

Crypto can be volatile and we don’t want to spam you, so the number of alerts you receive in a day may be capped or limited.  You will receive a maximum of two notifications when the price of the crypto you selected changes in a single direction within a 24-hour period. For this reason and because crypto price alerts may be delayed or disrupted (including due to market conditions and data outages), alerts should not be your sole way of monitoring and making decisions involving crypto.

 

Do I need to verify my identity or hold crypto before I can use price alerts?

No, anyone with a Venmo account can use crypto price alerts.

Crypto Transfers

Excited to move crypto in or out of your Venmo account? Look here for more info.  

 

How do I transfer crypto out of my Venmo account? 

You can transfer crypto in your Venmo account to other Venmo users, PayPal users, or external crypto wallets. First, you’ll need to verify your identity with Venmo, then, follow these steps:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Pick the crypto you want to send
    • Note: PayPal USD (PYUSD) can only be sent to wallets that support it
  3. Tap the transfer arrows in the bottom right corner
  4. Tap Send
  5. Enter the recipient’s information – you can search for then, enter a Venmo username, enter an external wallet address, or select from recent recipients
    • If you’re sending PYUSD, you may need to confirm the recipient’s wallet type
  6. Enter the amount of crypto you want to send and Venmo will calculate any relevant fees
  7. Review the transaction details
    • Remember: Crypto transfers cannot be cancelled or reversed. Be sure the recipient’s details are correct. Crypto sent to the wrong address will be lost.
  8. Hit Send to initiate the transfer

You can review your previous transactions in the feed on the Crypto tab.

 

What wallets support PayPal USD (PYUSD)? 

PayPal USD (PYUSD) is PayPal’s stablecoin. It can be sent to other Venmo users, PayPal accounts, most Ethereum addresses in wallets that support ERC-20 tokens, and most Solana wallets that support SPL tokens.  

If you’re planning to send PYUSD to a third-party wallet, please check to make sure that wallet supports PYUSD first.  

 

How do I receive a crypto transfer to my Venmo account? 

If you’ve verified your identity with Venmo, you can receive crypto transfers from other Venmo users, PayPal users, and external wallets:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Pick the crypto you want to receive
  3. Tap the transfer arrows in the bottom right corner
  4. Tap Receive
  5. You’ll see your crypto address QR code
    • If you’re receiving PYUSD, you can toggle between networks like Ethereum and Solana to make sure the address is compatible with the sender’s platform
  6. You can share your address with other people or they can scan the QR code

We’ll let you know by email when you receive crypto. You can always view the transfer status in your Crypto tab.

Make sure you check the crypto address type based on what is being transferred (for example, BTC should utilize a BTC address or ETH should utilize an ETH address). Crypto sent to the wrong address type (for example, sending BTC using a BCH address) will be lost.

Each time you receive crypto, your address will change for privacy reasons, previous addresses will be permanent and able to receive crypto.

 

Are there fees for sending or receiving crypto transfers on Venmo? 

You can transfer crypto to other Venmo and PayPal accounts without fees. There are network fees for sending it outside of the Venmo and PayPal network . 

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

How long does it take for a crypto transfer to process? 

Crypto transfers to other Venmo users happen right away. If you are sending crypto to an external wallet, it can vary from a few minutes to a few hours depending on the blockchain used and network congestion – the app will give you an estimated completion time when you initiate the transfer. 

In rare circumstances, an external transfer from a third-party crypto platform may not arrive by the expected time. In these cases, we recommend reaching out to the other platform to request a more specific timeline.

If a Venmo user receives a crypto transfer but they haven’t yet verified their identity, they’ll have 30 days to do so. Otherwise, the crypto will be returned to the sender. 

Recipients can verify their identity in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Pick the crypto you want to receive
  3. Tap the transfer arrows in the bottom right corner
  4. Tap Receive

If needed, you’ll be asked to verify your identity. 

If you’re unsure of the status of a crypto transfer, you can use the transaction hash in the confirmation email to check the status on your preferred blockchain verification website.

 

What is Ethereum Name Service (ENS) and how does it help me search for external crypto addresses?

Ethereum Name Service (ENS) is a domain naming system built on the Ethereum blockchain. It allows users to use recognizable names or words for their crypto wallet addresses, rather than the string of randomly generated letters and numbers of a standard Ethereum.

If you know your recipient’s ENS address, you can search for that instead of entering their wallet’s address.

 

Can I save crypto addresses to my Venmo account?

You can save crypto wallet addresses in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Address book
  4. Tap Add an address
  5. Enter the wallet address information and a nickname
  6. Tap Save address

When you send future crypto transfers, you’ll be able to enter a new wallet address, pick from recently used addresses, or pick a saved address.

If you want to manage your saved addresses, go to Address book in your crypto settings.

 

Can all Venmo users transfer crypto?

Venmo users must verify their identity in order to use crypto transfers.

Cryptocurrency FAQ

For all things crypto on Venmo, check out this FAQ!

 

What is Cryptocurrency?

Cryptocurrency is a digital or virtual currency that is created by Cryptography. It is not physical and not issued by a central authority. It utilizes a decentralized network and works through a distributed ledger technology, known as a Blockchain. 

 

What cryptocurrencies does Venmo support?

Customers in the US can buy, sell, transfer, and hold several different cryptocurrencies on Venmo:

  • Bitcoin
  • Ethereum
  • Litecoin
  • Bitcoin Cash

Venmo also offers PayPal’s stablecoin, PayPal USD (PYUSD).

 

Who can buy, sell, and hold cryptocurrencies?

In order to buy, sell, and hold cryptocurrency on your personal Venmo profile, you need to verify your identity with Venmo, including providing an SSN or ITIN. Learn more about verifying your identity with Venmo.

Cryptocurrency transactions are only available for personal Venmo profiles. They aren’t available on business profiles or charity profiles.

 

Are there fees for cryptocurrency on Venmo?

You will incur a fee when you buy or sell most cryptocurrencies on Venmo. The fee will vary based on the purchase or sale amount. Venmo displays that amount when you are making a transaction. You can learn more about how these fees are calculated in Venmo’s Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

If you use a linked bank account or debit card for a Venmo transaction, your bank may charge its own fees, like overdraft fees or insufficient fund fees. Venmo is not responsible for fees charged by your bank.

There are no fees to buy or sell PYUSD on Venmo.

You can transfer crypto to other Venmo and PayPal accounts without fees. There are network fees for sending it outside of the Venmo and PayPal network.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

  

I made a crypto transaction in error - can I cancel or reverse it?

There is not a way for you to cancel or reverse a crypto transaction.  

 

Can I transfer cryptocurrency into and out of Venmo?

Learn more about crypto transfers.

 

What are the limits?

You are limited to $20,000 in cryptocurrency purchases per week. There is also a limit of $50,000 in cryptocurrency purchases in a 12-month period. You are also limited to $25,000 per week in crypto transfers.

Learn more about other transaction limits on Venmo.

 

Why was my crypto transaction declined?

Crypto purchases on Venmo can be declined for several reasons:

  • You are limited to $20,000 in crypto purchases per calendar week
  • There is a limit of $50,000 in crypto purchases in a 12-month period
  • Your payment method could cause a decline, like if your debit card has a limit or insufficient balance
  • Your purchase can be declined by Venmo’s system if it raises a security flag. If this happens, the best thing to do is to try it again later

Crypto sales can be declined for similar reasons.

If you’re selling or transferring crypto that you recently purchased, we may need to wait for the original payment to settle. If this happens, the funds will either be released to your Venmo account in up to seven days, or you will receive a follow-up email from our team.

 

I want to know more about crypto

Check out the Crypto tab in the Venmo app for more educational material.

Cryptocurrency Security

Venmo works hard to help keep your crypto safe. Check out this article to learn more. 

 

Will Venmo protect my cryptocurrency?

Venmo works 24/7 to help prevent theft and fraud. If you suspect fraudulent activity on your account, please contact customer service immediately to report it and we will review your account activity. 

What we can’t protect you from are losses that result from cryptocurrency price fluctuations. Cryptocurrency is very volatile, and we suggest that you speak with a financial advisor or tax specialist to advise you. 

 

My account was taken over and cryptocurrency was purchased or sold without my authorization. What can I do?

Please contact customer service immediately to report it and we will review your account activity. 

 

Will I get a private key for the cryptocurrency in my Venmo account?

You own the crypto you buy on Venmo but will not be provided with a private key.

In case you are wondering, a private key is a person’s secret code to access and manage their cryptocurrency. If lost, stolen, or even forgotten, they can never get to their cryptocurrency again. If you can log in to your Venmo account, you will have access to the crypto balance you hold and can sell it according to our terms and conditions.

Holding Cryptocurrency on Venmo

Once you’ve gotten some crypto on Venmo, check out this page to learn what’s next. 

 

Does it cost anything to hold crypto on Venmo?

Venmo does not charge fees for holding crypto. Learn more about the fees for buying and selling crypto. 

 

How can I see what my crypto is worth?

After purchasing crypto on Venmo, you can track any price changes in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab
  2. Select your preferred crypto - we’ll update the displayed current price every few seconds

The Venmo app will display historical prices for your selected cryptocurrency, as well as your previous transactions with that cryptocurrency.  

By default, you will be shown the change in market value over a 24-hour period using a line graph. You can toggle to view one week, one month, six-months, one year, and the entire history of the selected cryptocurrency. When you tap on the timeline graph, you can view more specific values over time.  

 

What does the “Total Return” number mean?

Venmo reflects your crypto balance position with real-time returns based on current market conditions. This is reflected as “Total Return” in the Crypto tab of your Venmo app.

We calculate Total Return by finding the difference between the original purchase price of your crypto including fees, and the price it’s selling for right now. 

Total return and user entered purchase price will not be used for tax reporting. Learn more about taxes for cryptocurrency on Venmo.

 

Can I record a Purchase Price for my crypto?

Purchase Price refers to the price you originally paid for your crypto, including fees. After you buy or receive crypto you can enter a Purchase Price within the details of the transaction.

Entering a Purchase Price is optional to help give a more precise return figure. If you don’t enter a Purchase Price, we will use the price the crypto is trading for at the time you receive it.

Venmo is not responsible for the accuracy of any user-entered purchase price information. You will be responsible for the accuracy of the purchase price entered.   

Purchase Price can only be used for transactions recorded on Venmo. If you have crypto assets outside of Venmo and subsequently dispose of the assets, Venmo will not have visibility into your information, and it is, therefore, your responsibility to track and report such information.  

The Total Return and user-entered Purchase Price will not be used for tax reporting. Learn more about taxes for cryptocurrency on Venmo.

Scams involving crypto

As crypto continues to grow and innovate, so do the scams. Familiarize yourself with some of the common ways to protect yourself against fraud.

 

Protecting yourself from crypto scams

If you think someone is trying to scam you or you may have been scammed by someone claiming to be from Venmo, contact our Support team. We’ll never ask for your password or security code. We won’t ask for money, to send your crypto to an external wallet, for remote access to your account, or to install software.  

 

Double-check links, phone numbers, and emails

If something seems fishy, it probably is. Remember, scammers can also set up a phony website, caller ID, or email to seem legitimate, so, proceed with caution and be careful with links. If you get a call or text that you’re unsure of, paste the phone number in a search engine and check if it matches the caller ID. As always, with any questionable emails, don’t click any links. 

 

Investigate suspicious messages that look like they’re from Venmo

Scammers may call, text, or email you and pretend to be from Venmo. This can range from notification messages about a crypto transaction that you’re unfamiliar with to someone aggressively demanding payment for a crypto purchase. If you received a suspicious email that looks like it’s from Venmo, forward it to phishing@venmo.com and delete it from your inbox.

 

Protect your privacy

Don’t share banking details, personally identifying information, your financial status, or your private crypto keys with anyone online or on a website you’re not sure about. If someone asks you to install remote access software, don’t do it. This type of software can give a person complete access to your device and could allow them to steal your sensitive information.

 

Use caution with strangers

Some scammers will pretend to be celebrities, social media influencers, employees of legitimate companies or even romantic interests to steal your crypto. Be very cautious when sending money or crypto to people you’ve only talked to online, as they could be impersonating someone else.  

 

Do your research

Does it seem too good to be true? If someone contacts you about an investment opportunity, there’s a social media giveaway, or tells you to act fast—it could very likely be a scheme. If you’re curious about a new crypto project, do some internet sleuthing on the team, look at their marketing, review their white papers, and check their social media presence. Keep in mind that some of these scammers are extremely good at making themselves appear legitimate and gaining a following.  

 

Be sure you send crypto to the right person

Confirm you have the right crypto address of a trusted recipient before you send it. Using copy and paste or a QR code can help decrease the risk of typos. 

Much like the changing landscape of technology, there will always continue to be new scams. Staying vigilant and safeguarding your information can help keep you safe from crypto-related scams.  You can read more about common scams on Venmo and report scams on the government’s official website.

Selling Cryptocurrency on Venmo

Are you looking to sell some of the crypto you have on Venmo? This article will help.

 

How do I sell cryptocurrency on Venmo?

After you’ve bought crypto on Venmo, you can sell that crypto in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Crypto tab 

  2. Select your preferred crypto and we’ll show the current price – we update this every few seconds to keep you up-to-date 

  3. Tap Sell then enter the amount of crypto you want to sell

After your sale is complete, the funds will be available in your Venmo account. The money you receive from selling crypto is typically available in your Venmo account right away. Cryptocurrency sales are subject to review and could be delayed or stopped if there’s an issue.    

 

Are there fees when selling cryptocurrency on Venmo?

You will incur a fee when you buy or sell most cryptocurrencies on Venmo. The fee will vary based on the purchase or sale amount. Venmo displays that amount when you are making a transaction. You can learn more about how these fees are calculated in Venmo’s Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

If you use a linked bank account or debit card for a Venmo transaction, your bank may charge its own fees, like overdraft fees or insufficient fund fees. Venmo is not responsible for fees charged by your bank.

There are no fees to buy or sell PYUSD on Venmo.

  

I lost money on my cryptocurrency purchase. What can I do?

Venmo is unable to offer financial advice. We recommend speaking to a financial advisor.    

How long does it take to receive money from selling cryptocurrency?

It’s possible for your sale of crypto to take longer to process and it may not always be successful. If the sale fails, we’ll notify you that the purchase was not successful and your crypto balance will not change. You can try to sell again when you see fit.

Like other Venmo transactions, the proceeds from the sale of crypto can be held for review. If this happens, the funds will either be released to your Venmo account in up to seven days or you will receive a follow-up email from our team. You can see the estimated release date in your Venmo app. If your crypto sale has been pending for longer than expected, please contact our Support team.   

 

In what order is my crypto sold?

Venmo uses the highest-in-first-out (“HIFO”) method to calculate gains and losses when you sell crypto. More information is available in Venmo’s Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

Taxes for Cryptocurrency on Venmo

Learn more about what documentation Venmo provides to prepare for tax season. 

 

Will I pay tax on cryptocurrency profits?

You will need to report gains or losses from the sale of crypto on your taxes. For any tax advice, you would need to speak with a tax expert. You can find information from the IRS on their pages about Digital assets and Frequently Asked Questions on Virtual Currency Transactions.  

 

Will Venmo provide me any documentation for tax reporting?

Individuals who have sold cryptocurrency on Venmo during the tax year will receive a Gains and Losses Statement, irrespective of their state of residence.

Learn more about what tax documents you can expect from Venmo. 

 

I need to update my name on Venmo for tax purposes

Please contact our Support team for assistance.

Understanding PayPal USD

Want to learn more about Paypal’s stablecoin, PayPal USD? Check out this article to get the scoop! 

 

What is PayPal USD (PYUSD)? 

PayPal USD (PYUSD), is a type of cryptocurrency called a stablecoin and is fully backed by US dollar deposits, US treasuries, and similar cash equivalents. You will be able to buy and sell 1 PYUSD for 1 USD on Venmo and PayPal. 

 

What can I do with PYUSD? 

You can buy or sell PYUSD on Venmo. 

You can send it other people on Venmo and PayPal without fees. Plus, you can send it to other wallet addresses that support PYUSD (network fees will apply).  

 

Are the fees for PYUSD? 

There are no fees to buy, sell, or PYUSD. There also aren’t any fees for sending PayPal USD to other Venmo users or PayPal accounts.  

Network fees will apply when sending PYUSD to wallet addresses outside of Venmo or PayPal. Learn more about fees for buying, selling, or transferring cryptocurrencies on Venmo. 

 

I want to know more about crypto

Check out the Crypto tab in the Venmo app for more educational material.

Disputes

Canceling a Dispute

This article will guide you through how to cancel an ongoing dispute

 

How can I cancel my dispute?

You’re able to cancel your dispute at any time in the Venmo app.

  1. Go to the Me tab by tapping your picture or initials
  2. Tap the Settings icon near the top of the screen
  3. Tap Get Help
  4. Select Disputes
  5. Tap the dispute you want to cancel
  6. Select Cancel Dispute and follow the prompts

After completing the prompts, you will see a canceled tag next to your dispute.

 

I am having trouble canceling my unauthorized dispute.

Due to your original dispute having to do with potential unauthorized activity on your Venmo account, we’ll need to allow our specialized team to review your Venmo account for potential security concerns. You can track any updates to your dispute by navigating to the Disputes section in the app.

Dispute filing timeframes

You can file a dispute for any of the following reasons:  

  • Unauthorized activity in your Venmo account (a payment you don’t recognize or are confident you didn’t authorize).  
  • Purchase issue (you haven’t received an item or service or received an item/ service that was not as described).  
  • Billing errors or issues with subscriptions (you’ve been charged more than once, charged an incorrect amount).  

Timeframes for filing a dispute:

  • Item Not Received and Significantly Not as Described: Dispute must be opened within 180 days of the date you sent the payment to the seller.   

  • Unauthorized transactions: If your Venmo account statement shows transfers that you did not make, you should tell us at once.

  • Other Errors (not including Unauthorized Transactions): Dispute must be opened within 60 days after we provide you with your Venmo account statement. Notify us as soon as you can if you think your statement or receipt is wrong or if you need more information about a transfer listed on the statement or receipt. We must hear from you no later than 60 days after we sent the FIRST statement on which the problem or error appeared.  

You can read more about our various policies in the Venmo User Agreement.

Following up on a Dispute

This article will help view or submit more information for an ongoing dispute. 

 

How can I view my ongoing dispute?

You can check the status of your dispute by opening the Venmo app and navigating to the Disputes section of the app.

  1. Go to the Me tab by tapping your picture or initials
  2. Tap the Settings icon near the top of the screen
  3. Tap Get Help
  4. Select Disputes

Once there, you can view the details of any ongoing disputes. If you can’t find the status of your case on a transaction where you also reported an issue with your card issuer or bank, please contact our Venmo support team. 

 

How can I submit the requested information for my ongoing dispute?

If we’ve requested additional information, you can upload it in the Disputes section inside the Venmo app.

  1. Go to the Me tab by tapping your picture or initials
  2. Tap the Settings icon near the top of the screen
  3. Tap Get Help
  4. Select Disputes
  5. Select your specific dispute, and tap Submit on the prompt stating, “We need more information”

In this section, you can upload any documents that may benefit your dispute. You’ll also be provided the option to add any important details for this dispute.

If you have trouble checking in on your dispute, please don’t hesitate to contact us.

 

I need help understanding my completed dispute. 

If you are looking for more information about your resolved case, please contact us.

 

Why was the payment or refund I received placed on hold?

Your transaction is on hold due to an open dispute investigation. Placing payments on hold is a common industry practice that helps ensure a safer environment for users It can be concerning when funds are on hold for a payment you’ve received, but remember that having a payment on hold doesn’t mean you’ve done anything wrong.

To find out more about more details about your money on hold, follow the steps below:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Select transactions
  3. Locate the transaction with the On-hold tag, select the transaction

In the payment details, you will be able to see the clear reasoning for why your money is on hold and if there are any actions you need to complete before the money can be released. If there are no problems with the transaction, you’ll have the money available in your account once the hold is complete. You can track the details of this process within your Venmo app under your Transactions.

What does it mean I didn’t have sufficient money for an outstanding hold? 

At the time of the dispute, your balance did not have enough money to cover the total amount of the hold. When money is received in your account, we may apply those funds to your on-hold balance until your case is resolved.

Opening a Dispute

You can now open disputes on specific Venmo payments and transactions inside the app. Read on for details on how to open a dispute in your Venmo app. 

 

How do I open a dispute? 

Once you identify a transaction(s) that needs to be disputed, you can start a dispute inside the Venmo app.

Venmo Debit Card transactions and online purchases cannot be disputed or reported as unauthorized in the Venmo app. If you are unable to dispute a specific transaction, please contact our Support team to file the dispute.

When filing a dispute, choose the situation that best describes the reason for your dispute. Please attempt to answer every question with as much detail as possible.

Follow these steps to open a dispute:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Select the transaction you want to dispute
  3. Tap Need Help?
  4. Select the statement that best describes your situation
    • To include other unauthorized transactions, select Add more
    • After selecting your transactions, tap Add
  5. Tap Next
  6. Add any additional information
  7. Tap Submit Issue
  8.  If prompted, reset your Venmo password

Please provide as much detail as possible to ensure a smooth and timely resolution of your case. If you are having trouble disputing your transaction, don’t hesitate to contact Support.

 

How do I report a transaction error (i.e. unauthorized account activity or billing error) if I have already filed a case with my financial institution?

If you have already filed a case with your card issuer or bank related to a Venmo payment, please note that filing a case with your financial institution doesn’t constitute notice of an Error to us.   

You may also file a dispute for any unauthorized activity or billing error issues with Venmo. However, you may not receive a refund from both Venmo and your financial institution. 

If this transaction was paid using your financial institution and your Venmo balance, to file a dispute for any unauthorized activity or billing error issues on the amount paid using your Venmo balance, you will need to file a dispute directly with Venmo.
