To file a dispute, contact our Venmo support team.  

Make sure you file your dispute within the correct dispute-filing timeframe.  

Please note if you pursue a dispute with your card issuer for Purchase Protection related issues such as Items not received or items Significantly not as described, you cannot pursue a dispute/claim with us later. Contacting the seller is often the best way to solve a problem. They may be able to issue a refund or help with a return.  

 

Reporting an Unauthorized Transaction

If you believe your or your Teen User’s Venmo login information has been lost or stolen, please contact Venmo customer service immediately or call: 855-812-4430 or write: PayPal, Inc., Attn: Venmo Error Resolution Department, P.O. Box 45950, Omaha, NE 68145-0950.

Tell us AT ONCE if you believe that an electronic fund transfer has been made without your or your Teen User’s permission using your respective login information or by other means, or if your or your Teen User’s Venmo-activated mobile phone has been lost, stolen, or deactivated. You could lose all the money in your Venmo account. For suspected Unauthorized Transactions on Teen Accounts, you must report these to us following the instructions above.

Also, if your Venmo account statement or Teen Account statement shows transfers that you or a Teen User did not make, including those made with your (or a Teen User’s) Venmo login information or by other means, tell us at once. If you do not tell us within 60 days after we provided the statement to you, you may not get back any money lost after the 60 days if we can prove that we could have stopped someone from taking the money if you had told us in time. If a good reason (such as a long trip or a hospital stay) kept you from telling us, we may extend the time periods.

What type of documentation is acceptable for my dispute?

This article will help you identify what documents are accepted as evidence for your dispute. 

 

If you are a buyer, please utilize this table:

  • #### Dispute Type

    #### Evidence

  • Item not Received :

    • Additional item info:
      • Item name
      • Description
      • URL
    • Explanation of why the issue was not resolved in person (for items to be collected or delivered in person)

  • Significantly not as Described :

    • Explain the extent of the damage to the item
    • Explain how the item you received significantly differs from the item advertised
    • Original item listing vs photos of item received
      • A brief explanation of the difference between the items
    • Explanation of why the issue was not resolved in person (for items to be collected or delivered in person)

  • Credit not Processed :

    • Proof showing the seller promised a credit
    • Proof the seller is willing but unable to issue credit
    • Bank or CC Statement showing missing withdrawal

  • Duplicate:

    • Proof of multiple payments for same item or service

  • Paid by Other Means:

    • Proof of payment for the same item or service by other means

  • Incorrect Transaction Amount :

    • Bank or CC statement showing withdrawal amount does not match Venmo account
    • Receipt or invoice showing a different amount

 

If you are a seller, please utilize this table:

  • #### Dispute Type

    #### Evidence

  • Item not Received:

    Physical Items:

    • Provide tracking information
    • Provide conversations between you and the buyer

    Nonphysical Items:

    • Provided proof to verify the transfer of the goods or completion of the service
    • Provide a clear explanation as to which component or part of the evidence is relevant
      • Explain how it verifies the transfer of the goods or completion of the service

  • Significantly not as Described :

    Physical Items:

    • Original item listing (photos, URL)
    • Communication between you and the buyer showing terms of sale attempts to resolve, etc.
    • Evidence or explanation of why the item was delivered as described

    Nonphysical Items:

    • Provided proof to verify the transfer of the goods or completion of the service
    • Provide a clear explanation as to which component or part of the evidence is relevant
      • Explain how it verifies the transfer of the goods or completion of the service

  • Credit not Processed :

    • Proof the transaction was not canceled
    • Proof the item was not returned
    • Proof the buyer was credited through Venmo or another method

  • Duplicate:

    • Proof charges were for different items or services
    • Proof multiple items were sent to the buyer
    • Proof customer credited through Venmo or another method

  • Paid by Other Means:

    • Proof charges were for different items or services
    • Proof multiple items were sent to the buyer
    • Proof customer credited through Venmo or another method

  • Incorrect Transaction Amount :

    • Receipt or invoice showing a breakdown of charges matching transaction amount
    • Proof customer credited through Venmo or another method

Groups Accounts & Organization Accounts

Adding and editing group expenses

Looking to have members of a group help pay for something? Learn how to add or edit group expenses.

 

How do expenses work for Groups?  

You and your group members can add expenses to the group, then Venmo will simplify the math when it comes to making payments to each other.  

For instance, let’s say you, John, and Mary are going on a trip. You book a house for $1,500. Mary buys $150 worth of groceries, and John buys $45 worth of gas. You can each add these expenses to the group, and then Venmo can do the math to minimize payments between members. In this case, Mary would pay you $415, and John would pay you $520.  

You can also customize expenses to change the amounts due from each member, or to leave members out of specific expenses, like if John skipped a group dinner, or if Mary got a larger room in the house and is paying a little more for it.

 

How do I add an expense to the group?

You can add an expense to a group in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Add an expense  
  4. Enter the total amount of the expense at the top, then fill in the What’s this for field at the bottom 
    • You can adjust the dollar amount due from a specific member – just make sure the total adds up to the Total Amount you entered 
  5. Tap Add expense 

You can adjust the expense later, if needed, by tapping on the expense in the group’s feed. 

 

How do I edit an expense? 

If you need to make an adjustment to an expense that’s been added to your group, you can do that in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Group summary at the top of the activity feed 
  4. Tap on the expense in the Expense feed 
  5. Tap the pencil in the top right 

You can adjust the amount due from specific members, add or remove members from an expense, or delete the expense if it’s no longer needed.

Adding or removing members for a group

Do you need to add or remove members from one of your groups? Learn how to change who is in your Venmo group.

 

How do I add members to a group? 

You can add members to a group in the Venmo app:  

  1. Go to your Groups hub in the Me tab 
  2. Tap on the relevant group 
  3. Tap the Settings gear in the top right 
  4. Tap Add more people under the Group members list 

If you’re Venmo friends with the new members, they’ll be added right away.  

You don’t have to be friends with someone to invite them to your group, but if you aren’t friends on Venmo, the new member will receive an invitation rather than being added right away. That invitation will expire after 30 days. 

You can still add expenses to the group and involve pending members. When they join the group, they’ll be able to settle expenses or add additional expenses. You can edit or delete the expense while the invitation is still pending.

Remember, you can’t add more than 30 members to your group.

 

Can I add someone to my group if they aren’t on Venmo yet?

You can send your group link outside of the Venmo app, like in your group text thread:

  1. Go to your Groups hub in the Me tab
  2. Tap on the relevant group
  3. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  4. Tap Share a group link

If you send a group link to someone who doesn’t have Venmo yet, they’ll be asked to sign up for Venmo before they can participate in the group. They may also need to be approved by the group owner before they can join the group.

 

How do I remove a member of a group? 

You can only remove group members who are all paid up – that is, there’s nothing due to or due from them. You can check the group summary to see if there’s anything waiting for the person you want to remove. 

You also cannot remove the group owner. If the group owner wants to leave the group, they’ll need to transfer ownership first.  

If the group member is eligible for removal, you can do so in the Venmo app:  

  1. Go to your Groups hub in the Me tab  
  2. In the relevant group, tap the Settings gear in the top right 
  3. Tap on the person you want to remove 
  4. Confirm that you want to remove them 

If the removed member ever needs to rejoin the group, they’ll need to receive an invite from a remaining member.

Group splits and making group payments

Once your group has expenses added, you can settle payments with other group members. Read on to learn more.

 

What’s a Group Split? How do I make payments to the group members? 

Adding an expense to a group acts like record keeping, but it doesn’t move any money. When it comes time to make payments to group members, you can use the group split to make sure everything gets paid: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Pay at the top of the activity feed 

If you have money due to multiple people, you can review the specific amounts or remove people from this payment. Once you’ve paid everything due to group members, and once everyone has paid you, you’ll see a message that you’re all paid up at the top of the feed.  

 

I don’t understand how I ended up with this amount due to or due from me 

We calculate the amounts due to and due from you based on the expenses you’ve been involved with. If you’re unsure how you ended up with a specific amount, please review the group’s expenses: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Group summary at the top of the activity feed 
  4. Check the Expense feed 

Look to see if any expenses were edited or if you have any outstanding expenses you meant to add to the group.  

 

I have money due to me from a group. How do I remind members to pay? 

If you have money due to you from a group, you can remind members to pay in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Request at the top of the activity feed 

If you have money due from multiple people, you can review the specific amounts or remove people from this payment. When you hit Request, we’ll send a reminder to the relevant members.  

 

Someone paid outside of Venmo. Can I mark them paid? 

If an amount is due to or due from you, but the payment is settled outside of Venmo, you can mark it paid in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap Group summary at the top of the activity feed 
  4. In the Members tab, tap Mark as paid 
  5. Confirm you want to mark the payment as paid and tap Mark as paid 

Once you finish, you’ll see the relevant dollar amounts updated in the app.

Leaving or deleting a group

Has your group served its purpose? Learn how to leave the group or delete it altogether.

 

How do I leave a group?

Before leaving a group, you should consider hiding it instead. If you leave a group, you won’t be able to view any group activity or add any more expenses. If you hide a group, you can unhide it later if needed. 

If you’re sure you want to leave a group, please remember that you can only do so after you’ve settled all payments due to and due from members of that group, or removed yourself from those expenses. If you’re the group owner, you’ll need to transfer ownership before leaving.

Once you’ve taken care of those things, you can leave the group in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub in the Me tab  
  2. In the relevant group, tap the Settings gear in the top right 
  3. Scroll down to Leaving the group and tap Leave 
  4. Confirm you want to leave the group by tapping Leave group 

Once you leave the group, you won’t be able to view the group activity. You also won’t be able to add or edit any expenses.  

If you ever want to re-join the group, you’ll need to get an invite from a remaining member.  

 

How do I hide a group?

You can only hide a group if you don’t have anything due to or due from that group. If you’re all paid up, you can hide a group in the Venmo app: 

  1. Go to your Groups hub on the Me tab 
  2. Tap the relevant group 
  3. Tap the Settings gear in the top right 
  4. Tap Hide under Group visibility 
  5. Confirm you want to hide the group 

You can unhide the group later by tapping View hidden groups in the Groups hub, tapping that group, then tapping Unhide this group. 

 

How do I delete a group?

You can delete a group if you’re the owner and all expenses have been settled.  

If a group is deleted, no one will be able to review the group activity. No one will be able to add or edit any expenses.  

You can hide the group instead if you think you may need access to it in the future. You can also transfer ownership to another member and leave the group. If you’re sure no current or former members will need to the group, you can delete the group by tapping Leaving the group in the Groups settings.

Organization Accounts

The Organization Accounts product beta has ended, and we’re no longer accepting new Organization accounts. If you have an existing Organization account, you can still access it from venmo.com/organization-accounts.

Can I create a new organization account?

Venmo’s organization account beta has ended, and it is no longer possible to set up new organization accounts. While this beta has ended, you can explore Business Profiles and Charity profiles.

If you created an organization account before the product beta ended, you can continue to manage it from a web browser at venmo.com/organization-accounts

 

I need help with my existing organization account

What are the roles for an organization account?

Organization accounts have two separate roles:

Organization Owner

The owner is responsible and personally liable for all activity performed by the organization. There can only be one owner per organization, and this person can:

  • Add/remove managers
  • Add/remove payment methods
  • Utilize all other Venmo features (send/receive money, view transaction history, etc.)

Organization Managers

Managers can move money, but they can’t edit organization settings. There is a maximum of three managers per organization, and each of these managers can:

  • Send money from the organization account balance and payment methods previously added by the owner
  • Send charge requests from the organization account
  • Transfer funds from the organization account balance to a bank account previously added by the owner
  • View transaction history
  • See the last four digits of every payment method connected to the organization’s account, along with the bank name or card provider
  • Utilize other Venmo features

 

What does it mean when you say “the owner is responsible and personally liable for the organization account?”

If something goes wrong with the organization account, we need to know who to turn to. Because of this, every organization account has to have an owner who is responsible and liable for all activity performed under the organization account. This means that the owner will have to pay us back if the organization account ever incurs a negative balance.

 

Who can pay my organization account?

Anyone with a Venmo account can make payments to or receive payments from an organization – you don’t need to add people to the organization in order for them to transact with it.

If you’d like to make it easier for people to find your organization, you can ask them to send a friend request. Learn more about adding and removing friends.

 

How do I transfer ownership of an organization?

Only the organization owner can transfer ownership of an organization. The new owner must also have verified their identity with Venmo. If you are the organization owner:

  1. Sign into the organization account on the web
  2. Go to Settings
  3. Go to People
  4. Click Leave Organization
  5. Enter the information of the user you want to transfer ownership to

This user will receive an email asking them to accept the transfer. If they accept, you will be removed from the organization. If they decline, you will remain the organization owner. Any imported funding sources on the account will be unlinked from the organization account when the owner leaves.

 

Why can’t I access my organization on my mobile app?

In order to create the best experience, Venmo Organization Accounts are only available on desktop and tablet browsers. To log into your organization account, visit venmo.com/organization-accounts on the web.

 

Can I use the same funding source for my organization account and personal account?

Yes, the organization owner can import a payment method that is already linked to their personal account:

  1. Sign into the organization account on the web
  2. Go to Settings
  3. Click Payment Methods
  4. Click Import payment method

 

What is my organization’s sending limit?

Venmo Organization Accounts have spending limits that are tied to the organization owner’s personal account limits. Learn more about personal profile payment limits.

 

How do I leave an organization?

If you are the owner of an organization, there is currently no way for you to leave the organization other than by closing the organization account. Alternatively, you can transfer ownership of the organization to another manager and leave the organization once you’re no longer the owner.

If you are a manager, you can leave a organization:

  1. Sign into the organization account on the web
  2. Go to Settings
  3. Click Leave Organization

Once you leave the organization, you will not be able to make or accept payments on behalf of the organization.

 

How do I close/delete an organization?

Only the owner can close an organization account. To close a organization:

  1. Sign into the organization account on the web
  2. Go to Settings
  3. Go to Profile
  4. Click Close my Venmo Organization Account

 

Why can’t I add more than three managers to my existing organization?

Since managers can move money and change other attributes of the organization’s account, we have limited the number of managers each organization can add.

 

How do I receive SMS, push, or email notifications about activity on my existing organization account?

Only owners of the organization account will receive email notifications about financial and social activity related to the organization account.

There are no SMS or push notifications related to organization accounts.

 

How do I change my organization account’s email notification settings?

Organization account notification settings are tied to their owner’s personal notification settings.

Owners can change their email notification settings by signing into their personal account on venmo.com and navigating to Settings then Notifications. Any change made to your email notification settings will impact both your personal account and organization account.

Setting Up a Group

Looking to easily track expenses with a group of friends? Learn more about Groups.

 

What are Venmo Groups?

Groups are an easy way to manage, track, and split group expenses, like a group of friends going on a trip together or roommates splitting household expenses.  

Members can add group expenses. Then, Venmo will split things up and minimize the number of payments needed to cover what’s due to each person.  

 

How do I create a group?  

You can set up a new group in the Venmo app:  

  1. Go to the Me tab 
  2. Tap Create a Group at the top of the page or go into your active groups and tap Create a Group 
    • If this is your first time setting up a group, you’ll be given some information about how groups work 
  3. Enter a group name and pick a group emoji 
  4. Add members to the group 

You can add a maximum of 30 people to your group. Your Venmo friends will be added right away. If you add someone who isn’t your Venmo friend, they’ll need to accept the invitation before they’re added.  

When you create a group, you’ll be made the owner of that group by default. You can appoint a new owner if you’d like.

If you don’t see the option to create a Venmo group, please keep your Venmo app up-to-date as we make this feature available to more people.

Why Can’t I Join This Group?

Having trouble joining a group on Venmo, or were you removed from one? There are a few things that could be happening.

 

I tried to join a group, but I couldn’t join. Why not?

There are a number of reasons you may not be able to join a group on Venmo.

You need to be invited to join a group by a current group member. If your invitation is rescinded or expires, you won’t be able to join the group without a fresh invitation.

Groups are limited to 30 members, so if there are too many people already in the group, you won’t be able to join. You are also only able to be in 20 groups at one time.

If your Venmo account is restricted, you won’t be able to use the Groups feature. In these cases, you would have received a notification from Venmo about your account status. You can contact Venmo Support for assistance with your account.

Some Venmo accounts, like Teen Accounts or Business Profiles, are ineligible to join groups.

If you have blocked a group member on Venmo, or if another member has blocked you, you won’t be able to join the same group. You can still use other Venmo features and you may be able to join other groups.

 

I was removed from a group I was previously in. Why?

Other members of a group can remove you from that group. If you need to re-join the group, a current member would need to send you an invite.

You will be automatically removed from a group if your Venmo account is restricted. In these cases, you’d receive a notification about your Venmo account status. You can contact Venmo Support for assistance.

If you have blocked another Venmo user who is a member of the group, we may prompt you to leave the group as a way to fulfill our promise of limiting interactions with that user. If another group member has blocked you, you may be removed from the group.

 

I was removed from a group, but I had already been added to group expenses

If you were removed from a group, you can ask a remaining member to re-add you to the group. If they are able to add you, you can be re-added to relevant expenses.

If you aren’t able to be re-added to the group, you may need to send and receive payments with those group members via different means.

When you buy or sell cryptocurrency, we will disclose an exchange rate and any fees you will be charged for that transaction. The exchange rate includes a spread that Venmo earns on each purchase and sale.

Buying and selling cryptocurrency is subject to a number of risks and may result in significant losses. Please see our disclosure here for more details. Cash Back to Crypto Terms

These Venmo Cash Back to Cryptocurrency terms and conditions (“Cash Back to Crypto Terms” or “Terms”) are between you and PayPal, Inc. and will be effective as of August 10, 2021. Capitalized terms used but not defined in these Terms shall have the meanings defined in the Venmo Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

As a Venmo Credit Card Account (“Card Account”) Accountholder, you may be able to elect to automatically purchase cryptocurrency using the cash back rewards earned through your Card Account (“Cash Back”), as set forth below (“Cash Back to Crypto” or the “Program”). Your automatic purchase of cryptocurrency through the Program is also governed by the Venmo Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

When you have automatic Crypto Asset purchases turned on through the Program, you authorize Venmo to use your Cash Back to purchase the Crypto Asset you have selected in your Venmo app automatically upon the Cash Back’s transfer to your Venmo balance. Your Cash Back must be at least $1.00 in order to be used to purchase Crypto Assets. You may manage your preferences for Cash Back to Crypto, including by choosing a new type of Crypto Asset or opting out of the Program, by going to the Rewards section of your Venmo Credit Card section in the Venmo app.

Cash Back is typically transferred to your Venmo balance 1-3 days after the end of each Statement Period, as described in your Card Account Reward Program Terms. Venmo cannot tell you and you will not know exactly when the Cash Back will be transferred to your balance and your selected Crypto Asset purchased with the funds from your balance. Crypto Assets are inherently volatile. You should therefore check the exchange rate for your selected Crypto Asset in the Venmo Cryptocurrencies Hub at the end of each Statement Period and before the automatic purchase occurs.

The exchange rate at the time of the automatic Crypto Asset purchase will be displayed in your Venmo app transaction history and the email receipt for the purchase of Crypto Assets after the Crypto Assets have been purchased. You may cancel upcoming automatic Crypto Asset purchases by opting out of the Program in the Rewards section of your Venmo Credit Card section in the Venmo app at any time before your next Crypto Asset purchase.

Purchases of Crypto Assets made through Cash Back to Crypto will not be charged Venmo’s standard fee for buying cryptocurrencies. You will be charged a fee when you sell Crypto Assets, even if those Crypto Assets were purchased through Cash Back to Crypto. When you buy Crypto Assets through Venmo’s Cryptocurrencies Hub rather than through the Program, you will also be charged a fee. More information on pricing and fees can be found on the Venmo Fees page.

We make money and charge a spread when you buy and sell Crypto Assets, including purchases made through Cash Back to Crypto. For more details, please see the Venmo Cryptocurrency Terms and Conditions.

You received a notification “Yikes! Something went wrong!”

There is an issue with a payment from your Venmo account.  In good news, the receiver got the money, but we weren’t able to get the funds from your bank account.   

Because we couldn’t get the money from your bank, your Venmo account now has a negative balance, and your Venmo account is frozen.  This means that you will not be able to use your Venmo account. Unfortunately, your account will remain frozen until the negative balance is resolved.    

To determine the amount owed and pay the negative balance, you have several options: 

  • Open the Venmo app and make a payment
  • Log into venmo.com/recover from a computer
  • Call us at 888-674-2540

If you have any questions, please contact us at 888-674-2540.  We’re available Monday - Friday, 9:00 AM - 7:00 PM Eastern Standard Time. 

If you have already resolved the negative balance, please ignore this notification

Thank you, Venmo Collections

 

If you no longer wish to receive any push notifications from Venmo, please follow the below:iOS

Open your iPhone Settings -> Notifications -> Venmo -> Switch the toggle for Allow Notifications to off position

Android (Settings can vary by phone, for more information contact your device manufacturer)

Open your Android Settings -> Tap Apps & notifications -> Notifications -> Under “Lock screen,” tap Notifications on lock screen or On lock screen. -> Select “Don’t show notifications”.

This is an attempt to collect a debt, and any information obtained will be used for that purpose. Venmo is a service of PayPal, Inc. Venmo Collections is a department of PayPal, Inc.

If you’re receiving an error message in the Venmo app, check out this article for next steps. 

 

Error message with no specific instructions

If the error doesn’t include specific instructions, try these troubleshooting steps in order. They often resolve most issues: 

  1. Make sure you’re connected to the internet (Wi-Fi or data) and have enough storage/memory available on your device
    • If you are using a VPN, try turning it off to see if it resolves the issue
  2. Log out of your Venmo account and back in 
  3. Delete the Venmo app and reinstall it (This does not affect your Venmo account. It also ensures you’re using the latest version of the app.)  
  4. Make sure your device OS is up to date

If you’ve tried the above troubleshooting but the issue persists, please contact our Support team.  

 

400 / 403 errors

Usually, these error messages indicate you’re trying to do something in the Venmo app that isn’t possible or isn’t allowed. 

  • If you’re trying to send a payment or bank transfer, make sure you haven’t reached your limits by visiting our articles on payment limits and bank transfer limits.  
  • If you’re trying to log into your Venmo account, make sure you’re choosing “Sign In” instead of “Sign Up.” Remember that you can only log into Venmo from the U.S.  

  

“Bad state” errors

This error message means Venmo is or was experiencing technical issues, like an outage. If you tried to make a payment or bank transfer, check your transaction history in the app to determine if it was successful. If it failed, please try again later. 

 

What mobile operating systems is the Venmo app compatible with? 

Venmo is only able to support Apple iOS 14 or Android Lollipop (API 26) or higher devices at this time.

Holds

Why was the payment or refund I received placed on hold?

Placing payments on hold is a common industry practice that helps ensure a safer environment for users. There could be a few reasons for placing any money on hold. I know it can be concerning when money is on hold for a payment you’ve received, but remember that having a payment on hold doesn’t mean that you’ve done anything wrong. 

To find out more about more details about your money on hold, follow the steps below:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Select transactions
  3. Locate the transaction with the On-hold tag, select the transaction 

In the payment details, you will be able to see the clear reasoning for why your money is on hold and if there are any actions you need to complete before the money can be released. If there are no problems with the held payments you’ve received, you’ll have the money available in your account once the hold is complete.

Learn more about Buying and Selling holds 

Learn more about Tax Holds

Learn more about Dispute Holds

My outgoing payment is “Pending”

If you sent a payment to your friend’s phone number or email address and they didn’t receive it, the payment may be listed as “Pending.” Look here for more info.

When you send a payment to an email address or phone number that isn’t associated with an active Venmo account, the payment may show as “Pending” in your personal Transactions feed. This can also happen if the email address or phone number hasn’t been verified by the intended recipient.

This means your payment method will be charged for the payment you sent, but the money won’t reach the intended recipient until they add and verify the phone number or email address on their Venmo account.

You can view your Pending payments and requests in the Venmo app with the following steps:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap Transactions 

Any Pending activity will appear here, at the top of your Transactions feed. You can also access your pending payments on the web.

 

Help the recipient accept a pending payment or take it back

If your friend wants to accept the money, please have them follow the instructions to sign up or add an additional email address to their Venmo account.

If you want to take the money back by canceling the payment instead, follow these instructions:

  1. Go to the Me tab in your Venmo app
  2. Find the transaction at the top of your Transactions feed 
  3. Under the payment in question, tap Take Back 

Taking back a pending payment means that the money will be sent back to your original funding source. If you used a bank account or card, the money will be returned there. If you used your Venmo balance to fund the payment, the amount should be reflected in your balance.

Refunded payments should be reflected on your bank statement within 5 business days. Refund timelines depend on external factors and cannot be expedited.

Unknown Venmo Charges on Bank Statement

If you’re seeing Venmo on your bank or card statement and don’t have an account with us, someone else might have added it to Venmo. Read on for the next steps. 

Usually, this indicates your financial information was added to our service by an authorized friend or family member, or by someone else without your authorization.  

Before taking the steps below, it may be useful to check in with any family or household members who might have had permission to use your funding sources to send payments within Venmo (i.e., children, spouse, etc.). Venmo is popular with college students to split bills, rent, and other things easily with friends. For more information about what Venmo is, check out this article! 

 

I’ve confirmed these payments were unauthorized - help! 

If you confirm the payments were not authorized by you or someone with permission to use your card, it’s likely someone has gained unauthorized access to your personal and/or financial information. Unfortunately, there is no way for Venmo to determine how your account information was breached, but here are some steps we recommend taking as soon as you can:

  • Update your online banking and financial services passwords
  • Contact your bank/card company
    • File a dispute
    • Request a new bank account number or card number 

Once you let your bank or card company know the charges were unauthorized, they should be able to walk you through the dispute process. They are your best contact for this situation. They will reimburse you for your loss and will file a claim with Venmo separately for that amount.

Working with your bank or card company is the only way to ensure that your information is secure. Fraudsters often buy and sell personal information, so it’s likely your personal account numbers are still available for others to use outside of Venmo.  

 

Can Venmo refund me if I don’t want to contact my bank or card issuer? 

In these cases, we ask that you work directly with your bank to receive reimbursement for any unauthorized payments. Unfortunately, Venmo cannot take direct action on any Venmo account other than your own. 

 

Who used my information in Venmo? 

Due to our Privacy Policy we’re unable to provide you directly with information about any Venmo account other than your own. However, if you are interested in pursuing further action against a Venmo user you suspect is using your information in an unauthorized manner, please file a police report and have Law Enforcement contact us. Once we receive the necessary documentation, we’ll be glad to cooperate through the appropriate channels. 

 

How did this happen? How can I protect myself?

For additional information on how to protect your personal and financial information, please look over our article on personal security. This will help explain how your information was breached and how someone else was able to use that information.

What do I do if there’s a problem with my online purchase?

If you bought something online using Venmo but had an issue, check out this article for next steps.

 

Why was my online purchase declined?

You may not have had enough funds in your account, or we were worried that it was fraudulent activity. Learn more about payment declines. 

 

There is an unauthorized online purchase on my Venmo account.

If you have noticed an unauthorized charge on your account, please reach out to us. We will take the necessary steps to secure your account and open a dispute on the transaction.

 

 

I’m not satisfied with my online purchase. Who do I contact?

If you’re not satisfied with a purchase you made with Venmo through an authorized partner’s app or website, please contact the merchant first. They should be able to assist further.

If you are not able to come to a solution with the business, please reach out to us. While we don’t guarantee a refund, we’ll work with you to help you come to a solution. Your purchases may be eligible for the Venmo Purchase Program.

Learn more about the Venmo Purchase Program.

 

 

 

How long does a refund take?

The timing for a refund depends on the policy of the online merchant you purchased it from. Once the merchant issues the refund to us, it can take 1-5 business days to process. If you have any questions about your refund, don’t hesitate to reach out to the merchant.

 

I cancelled a subscription, but I’m still getting charged. What do I do?

Please contact the merchant. They should be able to assist further.

If you are not able to come to a solution with the business, please reach out to us. While we don’t guarantee a refund, we’ll work with you to help you come to a solution. Your purchases may be eligible for the Venmo Purchase Program.

Learn more about the Venmo Purchase Program.

 

Will refunds be credited to my bank account, card, or Venmo account?

In most instances, refunds will be credited to the same payment method that you used for the original online purchase. Sometimes, for various reasons, your refund will be credited to your Venmo account. To check what payment method you used for the original online purchase, navigate to your personal Transactions feed under the Me tab, then tap on the individual purchase.

 

Why was my refund more than my purchase? 

Your refund amount will vary by merchant. Some merchants can choose to provide a refund for more than the original purchase amount.

What do I do if there’s an unauthorized charge on my account?

Report unauthorized Venmo activity as soon as possible. Read this article to learn how.  Let us know if there’s an unauthorized transaction on your Venmo account as soon as possible.

For assistance, please contact our Support team. Be sure to include as much detail as possible in your request.  

If you are reporting unauthorized activity on your Venmo Credit Card, learn more about freezing your Venmo Credit Card, and please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462.  

Please look at the Venmo User Agreement for additional information about resolution procedures for unauthorized transactions.

Learn more about opening a dispute. 

What do I do if there’s an unauthorized charge on my Venmo Debit Card?

Report unauthorized Venmo activity as soon as possible. Read this article to learn how.  

 

There’s an unauthorized charge on my Venmo Mastercard ® Debit Card

If you are made aware of a transaction using your Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card that you did not authorize, first disable your card in the Venmo app. Go to the Cards tab check under the Venmo Debit Card section, tap the image of your card, and toggle to Temporarily disable card. 

If you do not have access to your app, you can call 855-204-4090 (24 hours a day 365 days a year) and deactivate your card that way.

Please note, you can deactivate your card at any time using the phone number listed above. If a live agent is available (8 AM - 8 PM CT) then you may notify an agent over the phone of any unauthorized charges and permanently close your card. Outside of that window, please contact us to notify us of the unauthorized charges and permanently close your card.

For additional information about resolution procedures for unauthorized transactions, please see the Venmo User Agreement and the Venmo Mastercard® Debit Cardholder Agreement. You can also view the Mastercard Guide to Benefits for additional details from Mastercard.

Payment Issues: Declines, Chargebacks, & Cancellations

Cancel Payment

If you’re having trouble with a payment you sent or received on Venmo, you may have some recourse. Read on for details on what to do about different payment issues.

 

There’s an issue with a payment I sent

The money will be available to the recipient right away, so there isn’t a way to cancel a payment once it’s sent. If you need help with a payment, there are specific steps you can take for certain scenarios:

  • Payment is pending: Take back the payment or have your friend verify their info. Learn about pending payments.
  • Payment sent to a stranger: Gather details about who you meant to pay and contact our Support team. Learn about accidental payments to a stranger.
  • Payment sent to the wrong friend: Ask your friend to pay you back or have them contact our Support team if they need help.
  • Accidental duplicate payment: Ask the recipient to pay you back for the extra payment. If you only sent one payment and see multiple charges on your bank statement, please contact our Support team.
  • Paid for a scam: Contact our Support team to report the issue. Learn about common scams on Venmo and how to avoid them.
  • Received payment from a stranger: Contact our Support team for help returning the payment. Do not attempt to pay the stranger back on your own. Learn about payments from strangers on Venmo.

If you need assistance from the Support team, you can contact us here.

Chargebacks on Venmo Payments

Notice of chargeback

If a Venmo user files a chargeback on a payment sent to you, we’ll let you know. This type of payment dispute is filed directly with a card issuer or bank and can happen if the sender doesn’t recognize or has an issue with a payment made with their debit or credit card. 

If you don’t agree that the chargeback is valid, you can help us dispute it by providing information about the transaction within 10 days of receiving this notification.

Chargeback disputes typically take around 30 days while we work with the card company, and it can take up to 75 days for them to come to a final decision. Please note that decisions on chargebacks are made by the card-issuing bank, not Venmo.

Providing info about a chargeback

If we have requested information regarding a chargeback filed against a payment you received, please send an email to with additional information to help us dispute the chargeback. This can include:  

  • Valid tracking information in order to dispute the chargeback received
  • Valid proof of a refund (either within or outside of PayPal) in order to dispute the chargeback
  • Additional evidence for dispute (other than tracking information)
  • Any communication between the sender and recipient
  • Any additional evidence or information
  • Accept liability for the chargeback

 

Filing a chargeback

When you file a chargeback with your card issuer, they will correspond with Venmo on your behalf about the case. For any updates on the case, please work with your card issuer directly.

In some cases, you may receive notice of a negative balance on your Venmo account, resulting from a chargeback. If this happens, your Venmo account will be temporarily suspended, and you’ll receive a notification from Venmo explaining what happened.

  • To pay Venmo back, you can visit venmo.com/addfunds from a web browser. After the payment posts to your Venmo account (this can take 3-5 business days), please contact our Support team so your account can be unfrozen.

Please note that filing a case with your financial institution doesn’t constitute notice of an Error to us.  You may also file a dispute for any unauthorized activity or billing error issues with Venmo. However, you may not receive a refund from both Venmo and your financial institution. 

If this transaction was paid using your financial institution and your Venmo balance, to file a dispute for any unauthorized activity or billing error issues on the amount paid using your Venmo balance, you will need to file a dispute directly with Venmo.

I accidentally paid a stranger on Venmo

If you accidentally paid the wrong person, don’t worry — this article can help.  

 

Can I cancel a payment I sent to the wrong person?

Payments on Venmo generally can’t be canceled once they’ve reached the recipient’s Venmo account, even if you accidentally paid the wrong person.

If you sent a payment to someone you know by accident, you can send that person a charge request for the same amount so that they can pay you back. Include a note explaining the mistake, and once they accept the request, the money will be added to your Venmo account. For added peace of mind, you can also reach out to that friend directly and explain the situation.

If you accidentally sent a payment to a stranger, please contact our support team and we’ll do our best to help.

When you reach out to us, please include the following information to help expedite the process:

  1. Username of the person that you sent the funds to
  2. Amount of the payment
  3. Date of the payment
  4. Username, phone number, and email address of the person that you meant to pay

If you sent a Venmo payment to an unregistered phone number or email address, learn more about what to do when your payment is listed as “Pending”

Note: Opening a dispute on these types of payments will not rectify this situation.

If this article didn’t help, learn more about what to do when you make a mistake in a payment you sent.

Payment Declined

Did you receive an error stopping your Venmo payment? Read on to find out why you may be having this issue.

 

Why won’t my payment go through?

A few common reasons for payments failing on Venmo are:

  • Your bank or card issuer is declining the transaction (outside of Venmo) 
  • The payment attempt has triggered one of Venmo’s automated security flags 

If you’re making a payment funded by a bank account, credit card, or debit card, reach out to your bank or card issuer directly (the fastest way to reach them is by calling the number on the back of your card). If your bank or card issuer is declining the transaction, Venmo cannot see specifics about the decline or override it. 

You can also try using a different payment method for the payment, like a different card or bank account. 

If you’ve checked with your bank or card provider, you can try making the payment again at a later time. If it’s urgent or if you continue to experience issues, you could try making this transaction on PayPal instead.

Payment from a Stranger

If you received a payment from someone you don’t know, read on to learn what to do.

 

Receiving a payment from a stranger

If you receive a payment from someone you don’t know, please contact our Support team. We can help you reverse this payment. If you receive a payment request from a stranger, you should decline it.   

To ensure this doesn’t happen again, you can block the user who paid you or sent you a request. We also recommend checking your privacy settings.    

Remember: When someone pays you on Venmo, that person does not have access to your payment methods (for example: your bank account information or your debit card number).

 

Does receiving a payment from a stranger mean my Venmo account has been hacked? 

If you haven’t received unexpected notifications from Venmo or notice any unauthorized transactions in your account, probably not. Because we offer a search feature for accounts, occasionally Venmo users may pay strangers by mistake (often with the same name as their friend). In those cases, it’s best to contact us to report that you were paid in error, then block the user to prevent the issue from happening in the future.

Cancelling a payment with someone you know

Learn about what to do if there’s a mistake in a payment with someone you know.

Payment In Review

As Venmo is a federally regulated money transfer service, there are times when we are required to review and collect information from our users to keep our platform secure.

In some cases, a check within the Venmo app may allow us to immediately collect the additional information we need from our users and speed up the review process.

 

My payment is under review. When will it be released?

Payments that have been flagged for review are typically resolved within 48 hours. If more information is required in order to process the payment, we will contact you via email.

Payment Methods: Banks & Cards

Adding a Bank Account

Check out this guide for help with adding a bank account to your Venmo profile.

To add a bank account to your Venmo profile from a computer, click here and select “Link Bank Account”. Then, follow the instructions on the page.

To add a bank account from the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the “Me” tab by tapping your picture or initials
  2. Go to the Wallet section
  3. Tap “Add a bank or card…” and then select “Bank”
  4. Select your preferred verification method

Please see this image for help identifying the account and routing numbers for your bank account:

There are a few important things to keep in mind when you add your bank account manually (using the account and routing numbers):

  • Do not enter the check number that appears after your account number. This can cause transfers to fail or be rejected by your bank.
  • When you add your bank account to Venmo manually, we’ll send “microtransfers” to your bank account and withdraw them to verify ownership (these will each be $1 or less). To avoid any overdraft or other bank fees, make sure you have at least $2 in your bank account.

Once you add a bank account to Venmo, we strongly recommend that you verify it before initiating any transfers. Venmo is not responsible for lost bank transfers as a result of incorrect bank credentials. Learn more about verifying your bank account on Venmo.

 

Why am I getting an error when I try to add my bank to Venmo?

If you’re trying to add your bank account using instant verification, make sure you’re using the correct login information. There’s a limit to how many times you can attempt to add a bank account using instant verification in one day, so you may need to wait until the next day to try again. 

You can also try adding the bank account using manual verification, but keep in mind it takes at least 1 full business day to complete this verification process (and you can’t make payments from the bank account until it’s verified). 

If you need to send a payment right away, try adding the debit card associated with your bank account instead.

 

My spouse/partner added our joint bank account to Venmo, can I add the same bank account? 

Learn more about adding a joint bank account. 

Adding a Credit or Debit Card

If you’re trying to add a debit or credit card to Venmo, check out this helpful guide. 

How can I add a card to my Venmo account from the web?

To add a card to your Venmo account from a computer, go to your payment methods then follow these steps:

  1. Click “Add bank or card”
  2. Select “Debit or credit card” then add your card information

How can I add a card to my Venmo account from the app?

If you’re on the Venmo app, follow these steps to add your card:

  1. Go to the “Me” tab by tapping your picture or initials
  2. Go to the Wallet section
  3. Tap “Add bank or card…” and then tap “Card”
  4. Add your card information manually or with your phone’s camera

Are there fees for using a credit card on Venmo?

Payments funded by credit cards are subject to our standard 3% fee. However, making purchases from authorized merchants does not incur a Venmo transaction fee, even if you use a credit card. 

  • See this article for info about purchasing online with Venmo
  • See this article for info about purchasing in stores

Some credit card providers also charge cash advance fees (possibly including an additional dollar amount or percentage rate, in addition to other possible cash advance service fees, including a higher APR) if you use your credit card to make payments to friends on Venmo. For more information about whether your card provider charges these fees, contact your card provider.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

I’m getting an error when I try to add my card

If you’re having trouble adding your credit or debit card to Venmo, the best thing to do is reach out to your card issuer to confirm that there are no issues. Oftentimes cards are declined because of a recent zip code or address change - your card issuer should be able to tell you more.

  • Note: You cannot transfer funds from your Venmo account to a credit card. To transfer to your bank you will need to add an eligible debit card (for instant transfers, a 1.75% fee with a minimum fee of $0.25 and a maximum fee of $25 is deducted from the transfer amount for each transfer) or a bank account (for standard transfers, no fee; for instant transfers, a 1.75% fee with a minimum fee of $0.25 and a maximum fee of $25 is deducted from the transfer amount for each transfer). 

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

How can I update the expiration date for my card?

There’s no way to edit or update just one piece of information for a card on your Venmo account. You’ll need to delete the old card from your account, then add the new/updated information as a new card.

Adding Money to Your Venmo Balance

Most users will see this feature after maintaining an active Venmo account for some time. If you’d like to access this feature in the meantime, you can sign up for the Venmo Debit Card.

 

How can I add money to my Venmo balance?

You can add money to your Venmo balance directly within the Venmo app.

Follow these steps to initiate the add money transfer:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap Add Money
  3. Select From a bank or debit card 
  4. Enter your amount and choose what payment method you want to use 
  5. Tap Add 

Once the transfer is initiated, you cannot cancel it. Here are just a few things to keep in mind:

  • You don’t need to have money in your Venmo account to make payments to other Venmo users. You can fund payments to other Venmo users using a linked payment method
  •  If you change your mind after initiating a transfer to your Venmo balance, you can transfer it back to your bank once it is completed. 
  • If your transfer to your Venmo balance fails, you will be notified via email, and the funds will not move from your bank to your Venmo balance
  • You can check the status of your transfer in the Venmo app, in your personal transactions feed under the Me tab  

What is the difference between adding money from a bank or a debit card? 

Adding money from a debit card is typically available instantly. 

Adding money from a bank typically takes 3-5 business days to reach your Venmo balance, and the funds from that transfer won’t be available until the transfer is completed on Venmo.

Adding money from a bank may:

  • Have its transfer time impacted by weekends or holidays
  • Create a completed charge in your bank account while the transfer is processing and before those funds reach your Venmo account

If the money doesn’t arrive in your Venmo account after 5 full business days, please contact Venmo Support. Transfers are reviewed, which may result in delays or funds being frozen or removed from your Venmo account. Learn more about Reviews of Transfers Into and Out of Venmo. 

 

I tried to add money with my bank account, and it was declined.

Before you can use a bank account to add money to Venmo, it needs to be verified. If you’ve already verified your bank account on Venmo, there are a few other reasons why your attempt to add money may be declined:

  • Your bank returned a notice of insufficient funds for your requested transfer. This information may not be up to date, so please try later or use a debit card instead.
  • You’ve hit your transfer limit for the rolling week. Learn more about Venmo transfer limits.
  • The transfer attempt triggered an automated security flag, resulting in a decline. There isn’t a way to override these declines, so you may need to try again later or explore other options.

Can I automatically transfer money to my Venmo balance?

Yes! You can set up automatic reloads for when your balance reaches the minimum amount you set, and then a reload for the amount you choose will be processed from the selected linked bank account or debit card.

Example: Add $50 from my linked debit card into my Venmo Balance when my balance falls below $25.

Transferring money out of your Venmo account through a Standard or Instant Transfer will not trigger an automatic reload.

How do I control my automatic reload? 

You can set up or make any changes to your automatic reload by going to the Me tab and following the steps below: 

  1. Tap Add Money
  2. Select Auto Reload 
  3. Select Reload Amount, and enter the amount you will want reloaded every time
  4. Tap Save
  5. Select Minimum balance, set the minimum balance you want to trigger the reloads 
  6. Tap Save 

You can come here to toggle off your automatic reload at any time. 

The Venmo Mastercard Debit Card is issued by The Bancorp Bank pursuant to license by Mastercard International Incorporated. The Bancorp Bank; Member FDIC. The Venmo Debit Card may be used everywhere Mastercard is accepted in the U.S. Mastercard is a registered trademark, and the circles design is a trademark of Mastercard International Incorporated.

Amex Send & Split®

American Express® Card Members can send money or split purchases with any other Venmo user.

 

What is Send & Split?

Send & Split is an American Express benefit that allows Card Members to send money to and split purchases with friends and family who have Venmo accounts. To use Send & Split, you must first enroll in the American Express® App, then link your Venmo account. Once linked, you have access to two distinct features: 

  • An Amex Send® Account to send money to any other Venmo user without the standard Venmo credit card fee
  • The ability to split your American Express Card purchases with any other Venmo users, and get paid back directly to your Card as a statement credit

Am I eligible for Send & Split?

Send & Split is available to American Express Card Members with a U.S. Basic Consumer Card in their American Express Online Account. Once enrolled, Card Members can also use additional American Express Consumer Cards with Send & Split. Prepaid Cards, American Express® Corporate Cards, American Express® Small Business Cards, and American Express-branded Cards or account numbers issued by other financial institutions are not eligible to be used with Send & Split.

 

How do I set up Send & Split?

Open the Venmo app, then head to the Me tab. From your Wallet, tap Add Bank or Card, then choose Amex Send Account. You’ll be prompted to sign into Amex and link your Venmo profile. 

Once connected, you can use your Amex Send Account as a Venmo payment method. 

 

How do you send money using Amex Send & Split?

Pay your friends directly from the Venmo app simply by selecting the Amex Send Account as your payment method. Just be sure to add money from your Card to your Send Account in the Amex app first.

 

How do I add money to my Amex Send Account?

Open the Amex app, tap the Account tab, scroll down to Payment Tools, and select Send & Split. Tap the Add Money button, choose an amount, then tap Add Money.  The amount you can add is subject to American Express approval, and the charge to your Card is not eligible to earn rewards.

 

Is there a limit to how much money I can add to my Amex Send Account? 

Yes. The amount you can add from each American Express Card will appear below the Card as Available to Add when you go to add money to the Send Account from the Amex App. This amount is determined by American Express. More information can be found in your Card Member Agreement with American Express. 

 

Are there limits to how much money I can send from my Amex Send Account? 

Yes. American Express limits the amount you can send to $10,000 per transaction. Your Send transaction will also be subject to a rolling 30-day Send transaction limit (this is separate and distinct from your rolling 30-day Person-to-Person transaction limit for each American Express Card, which only applies when you add money to the Send Account). Venmo’s transaction limits apply, and may limit the amount you can send. See the Personal Profile Payment Limits for details. 

 

Is there a fee to add money to my Amex Send Account from my American Express Card?

No, there is no fee to add money to your Amex Send Account. When you add money to your Send Account, it will appear as a charge on your American Express Card billing statement. The charge is subject to the APR for purchases, just like any other purchases on your Card account based on the terms of your American Express Card Member agreement. This is not a cash advance. The charge to your Card does not earn any rewards.

 

Is there a fee to send money using the Amex Send Account?

American Express and Venmo do not charge a fee to send money using your Send Account.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

Can I use the Amex Send Account to pay for goods and services?

No. The Amex Send Account should not be used to pay for goods and services, and making such payments is a violation of the Amex Send Account Terms and Conditions. You should only send money to friends and family that you know and trust.

 

What if I send money to the wrong person?

If you send money to a phone number or email address that is associated with a Venmo or PayPal account (the send transaction appears in the Complete section within the Send tab of the Amex App), the money is made available to the recipient right away. You will not be able to cancel this transaction. Please contact Venmo immediately to ask for assistance. As a reminder, you should only send to friends and family that you know and trust. 

 

Can I transfer money from my Amex Send Account back to my American Express Card?

Yes. To transfer money from your Amex Send Account back to your Card, go to the Account tab in the Amex App and select Send & Split. Within the Send tab, tap Manage Balance and then Transfer to Card. Enter the amount, select the Card, and tap Done. A credit for the amount you transferred will post to your Card account within 24-36 hours.

 

How does the split purchase feature work?

Open the Amex App, choose any pending or posted charge that appears within your current or most recent billing cycle, and tap Split It. Select up to 20 contacts who you want to split the purchase with, and we’ll divide the total purchase amount evenly. You can then adjust the total split amount or customize any of the individual request amounts. 

Choose to get paid back directly to your American Express Card account as a statement credit or to your linked Venmo account. The contacts you chose to split with will get a request notification via Venmo, just as they would for other Venmo requests.

If you choose to get paid back to your Card, you’ll receive an American Express notification when a request is accepted and the money will be applied as a statement credit to your Card account that will post within 24-36 hours. If you choose to get paid back to your Venmo account, Venmo will notify you when you receive the payment.

You are still responsible for the full purchase amount charged to your Card and will keep any rewards that you earn for the purchase that you split.

 

Is there a fee to split a purchase? 

No, there is no fee to split a purchase. You are still responsible for the full purchase amount that you split, and any interest or fees we assess on that purchase based on the terms of your Card Member Agreement with American Express.

Bank Accounts & Cards - FAQ

Where can I see my payment methods on Venmo?

If you’re on the Venmo app, go to the “Me” tab and check the Wallet section. You should see a list of the bank accounts and cards that you’ve added to your Venmo account. 

Can I use a prepaid card or gift card on Venmo?

We allow credit, debit, and prepaid, network branded (e.g. American Express, Discover, MasterCard, Visa) cards registered under your name to be added to Venmo. Please note the following:

  • Cards may be declined by the card issuer or Venmo for funds availability or fraud prevention reasons
  • Some payment cards may require a ZIP code to be linked to your account

What about credit cards?

Some credit card providers charge cash advance fees (possibly including an additional dollar amount or percentage rate, in addition to other possible cash advance service fees, including a higher APR) if you use your credit card to make payments to friends on Venmo. For more information about whether your card provider charges these fees, contact your card provider.

Learn more about cash advance fees on Venmo.

Are there fees to use Venmo?

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

Can I change where my Venmo payment is coming from?

If you’ve already sent your payment, no. There’s no way to change the payment method or amount of your payment after it’s been sent. This is because the moment you send a payment on Venmo, the funds are authorized to be withdrawn from your payment method.

If you have access to a Venmo balance and have enough balance to cover the entire cost of your payment, Venmo will use your balance to fund your payment. Otherwise, Venmo will charge the entire cost of the payment to your preferred payment method.

There is one exception to this rule:

  • If you sent a payment using iMessage and it hasn’t been accepted yet, or it went to a “New User,” you will still have the option to cancel the payment
  • You can read more about that here

Please see the article on how to update your payment method, so that the correct payment method is debited for next time!

 

Can a bank account or card be added to two separate Venmo accounts?  

If you share ownership of a bank account or debit card with another Venmo user, you may both be able to use the payment method on Venmo. (Please note that the payment method can only be linked to two Venmo accounts at a time, so any additional users who attempt to add it will be declined.)

For more information about joint bank accounts, visit this article. For more information about shared debit cards, visit this article.

At this time, credit cards cannot be used on more than one Venmo account.

If you’re unable to add a payment method because it’s already linked to another Venmo account, feel free to contact us so we can help.

 

Why was my card charged $0/$1 when I added it to Venmo?

Sometimes when you add a card to Venmo, you’ll see a small line item on your debit or credit card statement that looks like this:

CHECKCARD XXXX VENMO 646-863-9557 NY 000000000000 $1.00

The amount could also be $0, but it’s never larger than $1. This is a temporary authorization that we run on your card to confirm that the details you entered are correct. It’s important to note that this is not an actual charge to your card. After we create this authorization, we cancel it so that you can use this money again.

If you still see a $1 authorization from Venmo after 5-7 business days, please contact your bank and ask them to remove it for you. We already attempt to cancel all of these when we create them, and cannot take further action on our end once we do so.

 

Can I add my PayPal card or PayPal account to Venmo? 

Adding your PayPal card or PayPal account to your Venmo account is not available at this time.

 

How do I remove a bank account or card?

You can delete a payment method in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Go to the Wallet section
  3. Tap on the payment method you would like to remove
  4. Tap on Remove and then tap Remove on the pop-up to confirm

If you run into an error message, make sure the payment method isn’t set as a backup payment method or a preferred payment method.

 

Why is Venmo telling me to reconnect my bank?

If you receive an error message that says, “We lost connection with your bank,” you’ll need to delete the bank account from your Venmo account and add it back again. This can happen if you update your online banking password.

If you re-add the bank account manually (using your account and routing number), you’ll need to verify it. This process is usually completed within one business day, but it can take up to three business days in some cases. 

If you need to make a payment urgently, consider adding a debit card to your Venmo account.

Card Limit

For security reasons, Venmo places a limit on the number of cards a user can add to their account.

Users can have up to 4 active or deleted cards in their account at any given time over a 6 month rolling period.

The 4 card rolling limit means that once you add a card to Venmo, it will count against your card limit for 6 months, even if you delete it. So, a card applies to this 4 card limit if it is was added fewer than 6 months ago (even if it has since been deleted).

If you are seeing an error message that you’ve reached the limit, you are likely hitting the rolling limit and will need to wait until it’s been 6 months since you first added a card. At this time, Venmo is not able to increase your limit above 4 cards. Additionally, we (Venmo) may change this limit at any time in our discretion.

Cash a Check FAQ

Due to a change outside of Venmo, the Cash a Check feature is no longer available for users in the state of New York starting on February 14th, 2022. We regret any inconvenience this may cause and encourage you to contact our Support team, should you have any questions about this.

Please note, any checks submitted by users in New York prior to 2/14/22 will still be processed.

 

How do I use the Cash a Check service in the Venmo App?

You can cash a check to your Venmo account in the app by going to the Me tab and tapping the Add Money button, then following the steps below.

  1. Tap Cash a Check
  2. Enter the check amount and take a picture of the front and back of your endorsed check with your mobile device.
  3. Once you’ve filled in your info, tap Next. 

Then, select how quickly you want to have access to your money (fees may apply).

  • It usually takes a few seconds to review a check for approval, although it can take 10-15 minutes and in rare circumstances, up to 1.5 hours to verify enough check information to approve your check. 

If your check is approved, you’ll be prompted to write “VOID” across the front of your check and submit another photo showing it has been voided. Do not write “VOID” on your check until you are prompted to do so, after you receive confirmation that your check was approved. 

Once finalized, you’ll see a confirmation page verifying the amount of the money transferred that will be available in your Venmo account. 

 

When will my money be available if I use the Cash a Check service in the Venmo app?

When you submit a check through the Cash a Check feature, and your check is approved, you can choose one of two options on the “Review Check Details” screen to get your money. 

  • “In minutes” option:  You can choose to pay a fee and get your money in minutes. It usually takes a few seconds to review a check for approval, although it can take 10-15 minutes and in rare circumstances, up to 1.5 hours while we work to verify enough information to approve your check. If your check is approved, your money can be credited to your Venmo account usually within minutes.
  • “In 10 days” option: If you’re not in a rush, you can choose to pay no fee. The money can be available in your Venmo account in 10 days if the check is not returned unpaid within the 10-day period.

** What are the limits on Venmo-Ingo Cash a Check?**

You may cash checks ranging in value from a minimum of $5 to a maximum of $5,000. You can cash multiple checks totaling up to $5,000 in value per day and up to $15,000 in value per month. All checks are subject to review for approval. 

Is my data secure when using theCash a Check service in the Venmo app?

Your information is stored in Ingo Money Inc. systems designed to prevent unauthorized access by others and your personal information is only shared as set forth in the Ingo Money Privacy Statement. 

How do I take a picture of a check using the Cash a Check service in the Venmo app?

Cash a Check features automatic image capture, so taking pictures of your checks is easy! You can follow these steps to take your pictures:

  1. Place your endorsed check on a dark-colored, plain surface that is well-lit
  2. Open the Venmo app, go to the Me tab
  3. Tap Add Money in the Wallet section
  4. Tap Cash a Check
  5. Tap the first check image field and position your mobile device over the check at a slight angle so that you don’t cast a shadow on the check
  6. Fit the corners of the check inside the guidelines and watch the box turn green
  7. Turn the check over and tap the second check image field to repeat the process and take a photo of the back of the check
    • If auto image capture moves too quickly for you, or your device doesn’t support it, you can take a picture manually by tapping the camera and flash icons on your screen. 
  8. Confirm that the amount is correct and tap Next.

Continue to follow the screen prompts to the Review Check Details screen where you can choose to receive your money “in minutes” (fees may apply) or “in 10 days” (no fee). Review your transaction details on the Confirm screen, then submit the check for review. 

What are the fees to use the Cash a Check service in the Venmo app?

  • Get Money in Minutes for a Fee: To cash a check and get your money in minutes, the standard fee is 1% for payroll and government checks with a pre-printed signature and 5% for all other accepted checks, with a $5 minimum fee per check.
  • If your check isn’t approved to be cashed, no fees will be assessed.
  • Get Money in 10 Days for No Fee: Alternatively, you can choose to get your funds in 10 days for no fee. Your money will be added to your Venmo account if your check is approved and is not returned unpaid during the 10-day period.

I’m using the Cash a Check service in the Venmo app and my check wasn’t approved. Why?

All checks submitted are subject to review for approval by Ingo Money, and most of the checks reviewed are approved. There are times when we are unable to approve a check due to inconsistencies in check images, limit-related issues, insufficient check verification data, or information about the check writer’s account. Unfortunately, we can’t always tell you exactly why a check is declined because privacy regulations prevent Ingo from sharing a check writer’s account information with you.

Examples of why a check may be declined: 

  • The name on the check doesn’t match your name, as it appears on your Venmo account. 
  • The check wasn’t endorsed. 
  • The amount of the check is less than $5 or more than $5,000, which are the check cashing limits set by Ingo Money. 
  • The amount of the check will cause you to exceed the Cash a Check limits. You may use this service to cash checks ranging in value from a minimum of $5 to a maximum of $5,000. You can load multiple checks totaling up to $5,000 in value per day and up to $15,000 in value per month. 
  • The check images are illegible or do not pass authenticity screening. 
  • The check is a single-party check (drawn on another account you own), a temporary check, a credit card check or another unusual check type we don’t support. 
  • A properly voided image of the check was not received. 
  • Sufficient information could not be verified on the check writer’s account. 
  • The check was written on a closed or compromised account.

If your check is declined, you’ll be notified in the Venmo app. The funds will not be added to your Venmo account, and no fee will be assessed.

You may attempt to deposit or cash your check with another service provider, or alternatively contact the check writer and request a new check or a different method of payment. If you have additional questions, please reach out to Ingo directly.

Cash Advance Fees

What are cash advance fees?

Cash advance fees are the same fees that are charged when you use a credit card to withdraw money from an ATM or branch. The interest rate for this type of transaction is usually higher than using the credit card for a purchase.

When you send money to a friend on Venmo, your card issuer may charge an additional fee (either a fixed dollar amount or a percentage rate–whichever value is higher). They might also charge other cash advance service fees including a higher APR.

Things could add up! Be sure to check with your card issuer about the fees they may charge.

 

Which credit cards charge cash advance fees?

The only way to be certain whether or not your card issuer will charge a fee is to ask them directly. If we think your credit card could charge an extra fee, we try to warn you before you make a payment on Venmo.

 

Why can’t Venmo show the exact value of the cash advance fees I might be charged? 

Your credit card issuer decides all of the fee details, and this info isn’t shared with us when you’re sending money on Venmo. You can check with your card issuer to learn more about the fees they might charge.

 

How can I avoid cash advance fees on Venmo? 

The easiest option is to use a bank account or debit card for payments you send on Venmo. These payment methods typically don’t incur a fee.

Changing Your Payment Method

Before completing a payment on Venmo, you can edit your payment method to change the bank or card funding the transaction. Here’s how:

  1. Confirm the amount and note for your payment, and select the blue Pay button
  2. On the next screen, tap on the bank or card shown at the bottom (your Venmo balance may already be selected)
  3. Select the payment method you want to use

Updating your payment method for a single payment will also change your default payment method for future payments to friends. If you have access to a Venmo balance and have enough balance to cover the entire cost of the payment, Venmo will use your balance to pay for your payment (and you won’t be able to select a bank or card instead). Otherwise, Venmo will charge the entire cost of the payment to your preferred payment method.

 

I need to change the payment method for a payment I already sent

It’s not possible to change the payment method used after sending a payment. The moment you send a payment on Venmo, the funds are authorized to be withdrawn from the selected payment method and made available to the recipient.

We always recommend double-checking your payment method before sending a payment.

Direct Debit FAQ

Learn more about using your Venmo balance for one-time and recurring subscription payments. This feature is not yet available to all Venmo customers.

What is Direct Debit? 

Direct Debit is a convenient way to handle one-time and recurring payments using your Venmo balance. Direct Debit authorizes a merchant (or service provider) to initiate payments from your Venmo Balance account for things like utilities, subscriptions, or memberships.

These Direct Debit payments are set up and authorized on a third-party merchant’s website or mobile app. Direct Debit payments occur over the ACH network using your assigned Bancorp account and routing number.
