P2P Referrals FAQ + Terms

How does Refer a Friend work? 

Venmo is currently offering the opportunity for users to earn a reward for referring their eligible friends. Additionally, friends who use the referral links can earn a $5.00 reward. 

Both you and your friend can earn $5.00 through the referral program if the following criteria are met: 

  • Your friend makes a qualifying personal payment. In order to qualify, the payment must meet three simple criteria:
    • It must be at least $5
    • It must be funded by a bank account or card
    • It must be made to another user within 14 calendar days of the referral link creation

Note:  Referrers can earn up to 10 referral rewards (for a maximum total of $50.00 in referral rewards). Additional terms apply.

 

Who can I refer?

You can refer eligible friends, including:

  • Friends who have never had a Venmo account
  • Friends who have a Venmo account, but have never sent or received a personal payment

 

How many referral rewards can I get by inviting my friends? 

As a referrer, you can earn up to ten $5.00 rewards (for a maximum $50.00) when your friends complete the criteria for the reward. 

Additional terms apply.  

 

How fast do I get my reward after my friend signs up and makes a payment? 

If your eligible friend taps your referral link and makes a qualifying payment, you should receive your reward in fourteen days, but may take up to a month (if you haven’t already received the maximum number of rewards). Your friend’s payment needs to meet three simple criteria:

  • It must be at least $5
  • It must be funded by a bank account or card
  • It must be made to another user within 14 calendar days of the referral link creation

Depending on your notification settings, you’ll receive a notification when you receive your reward. 

 

How can I share my link with a potential referee? 

In the Venmo app, go to the Me tab, and then tap Invite friends, earn $5.

From there, you can choose to share your link by message, email, social media, or have the option to manually paste your link into a different communication of your choice. You must only share your link with people you know. You may never publicly post it in group forums that are likely to include people you do not personally know.

 

My friend referred me, how do I sign up and get my reward? 

Your friend should have provided a referral link via email, text message, or social media. Once you click the link, sign up for a Venmo account, and make a qualifying payment, you’ll receive $5.00 in your Venmo account. That qualifying payment needs to meet three simple criteria:

  • It must be at least $5
  • It must be funded by a bank account or card
  • It must be made to another user within 14 calendar days of the referral link creation

Note, if you don’t already have a Venmo account, you’ll need to create one after you click the link. If you signed up without clicking the Referral link, just be sure to tap on the link before making a qualifying payment. 

 

Terms and Conditions

By participating in the Venmo referral promotion (the “Program”), you agree to the following terms and conditions (“Terms”). Venmo may at any time and in its sole discretion, without prior notice, terminate, cancel, suspend, or modify the Program or these terms. If you are the person inviting friends to participate, you are a “referrer” under these terms, and if you are a person who receives an invitation, you are a “referee.” 

Eligibility and Overview 

  1. You may participate in this Program as a referrer until the Program is terminated or modified by Venmo. If you refer a referee prior to Venmo’s termination or modification of the Program, you and such referees will still be eligible to earn the reward described below even if the referee does not fulfill the Reward Criteria (defined below) before such termination or modification. 
  2. To be eligible to earn rewards through the Program, each referrer and referee must sign up or have a Venmo personal account in good standing (“Valid Account”) at the time of participation through when his/her reward(s) are issued.  Venmo Business Profiles, Charity Profiles and Venmo Teen accounts are not eligible for this Program.
  3. A referrer must receive an email, in app banner, or view a social post from Venmo inviting participation in the program (“Notification”). 
  4. A referrer may refer a referee by sending them an email, text, or social post that includes an invitation link to participate in the Program, as provided by Venmo (“Invitation”). Only Qualified Referees are eligible referees for this Program. “Qualified Referees” is (1) a person who has never had a Venmo account or (2) has a Venmo account but has never sent or received a personal payment (as the term is used in the Venmo User Agreement). Each Qualified Referee must complete all the following steps (“Reward Criteria”) within fourteen (14) days of the date on which the referrer’s referral link was created Pacific Time).
  5. Sign up for a Valid Account for the first time via the Invitation sent by the referrer if the Qualified Referee has never had a Venmo account. Signing up for a Valid Account may require certain actions to verify the Valid Account and is subject to the Venmo User Agreement. 
  6. Link a bank account, credit card or debit card to their Venmo account (as applicable); and
  7. Use the “Pay or Request” functionality to make a personal payment of $5 or more via the Venmo using their linked bank account, credit or debit card. 

 

Limits on Referrals: Referrers may only refer family and personal friends and acquaintances. Additionally, you must not publish or distribute the Invitation link or Notifications received from Venmo containing the link online or otherwise where the audience is likely to include people who are not your family or personal friends/acquaintances. If you do so, Venmo may disqualify you from participation in the Program and any successful referrals will be void, in Venmo’s sole discretion. There is a limit of one (1) reward per referee and ten (10) rewards per referrer. If two or more referrers invite the same referee, Venmo will credit the reward to the person who has their Invitation accepted first, as determined in Venmo’s sole discretion. If a referrer has reached their limit of 10, all referrals made prior to reaching the limit will still be eligible to earn their reward provided all required steps are met and these Terms are otherwise complied with.

Reward: For referrals successfully completed by December 31, 2024, at 11:59:59 p.m. Pacific Time, the reward amount as defined in the Notification will be sent to a Valid Account within fourteen days (14) days but may take up to thirty (30) days. 

Receiving and Using Rewards: 

  • Venmo reserves the right to limit, cancel, delay, or revoke a reward if Venmo determines, in its sole discretion, that an Eligible Participant has violated these Terms or the Venmo User Agreement or has acted in a fraudulent or abusive manner, or if Venmo restricts or closes their Valid Account pursuant to the Venmo User Agreement. 
  • Venmo may provide an alternate reward of equal value if it is unable for any reason to fulfill the reward. Reward redemptions are final, will not be returned, and are subject to review and verification. 
  • Venmo is not responsible and/or liable if any e-mail, reward, referrals, or Program-related materials or correspondence are lost, fraudulent, abusive, stolen, late, incomplete, illegible, interrupted, delayed, altered, defective, misdirected, tampered with, or irregular in any way or if any participant’s e-mail address, Valid Account, or other contact information does not work, is deleted, or is changed without participant giving prior written notice to Venmo. 
  • Program is void if Eligible Participant’s accounts or transactions are not completed through legitimate channels, in violation of these Terms, or irregular in any way. 

Governing Law: This Promotion is governed by California law. By entering, entrants agree to be bound by these terms and conditions and the Venmo User Agreement. Any questions relating to the offer will be resolved in Venmo’s sole discretion and its decisions related to the offer will be final and binding. 

Sponsor: Venmo, a PayPal, Inc. service, 117 Barrow St., New York, NY 10014.

What is Venmo?

Venmo is a great way to pay people, get paid, grow a business, and more. Learn more about Venmo. 

 

What is Venmo for? 

Venmo allows you to pay and request money from your friends. At its core, Venmo provides a social way to pay your friends when you owe them money and don’t want to deal with cash. For example:

  • Splitting a lunch bill
  • Paying your friend half of a cab fare
  • Sending your roommate your half of the rent

You can find out more information here.

Venmo can also be used to make purchases with authorized merchants. For example:

  • Using the Venmo Mastercard Debit Card anywhere in the U.S. where Mastercard is accepted
  • Using Venmo as a payment method in authorized partner apps or mobile websites 
  • Using in-store QR codes to complete purchases with Venmo
  • Buying something from a Venmo Business Profile
  • Tagging a Venmo payment as a purchase in the Venmo app

Venmo should not otherwise be used to make or accept payments for goods and services with people you don’t know (for example, concert tickets, electronic equipment, sneakers, a watch, or other merchandise, deposits for apartments, or dog walking). Learn more about using Venmo to buy or sell merchandise, goods, or services. 

Venmo balance

A more detailed explanation of how to access and use a Venmo balance can be found here.

 

Payment methods

When setting up your Venmo account, one of the first things you do is decide how you want to pay people, also known as setting your payment method. In Venmo, you can pay people from any of the following:

  1. Your Venmo balance (if you have access to a Venmo balance)
  2. A credit/debit card (imagine being able to pay a friend directly from your credit card)
  3. A U.S. bank account 

For more info on how to add funding sources, please see our articles about adding bank accounts and adding cards to your Venmo account. 

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

Bank transfers

You can transfer money from your Venmo account to your bank after connecting your bank account to Venmo. Bank transfers submitted before 7 PM ET will typically be available in your bank account the next business day, excluding weekends and bank holidays. Transfers are reviewed which may result in delays or funds being frozen or removed from your Venmo account. Learn more about bank transfer timelines.

Venmo should not be used to transfer money between your own bank accounts. Venmo is designed to be a peer-to-peer payments app that you can use to pay friends, local businesses, charities, and more.

If you need to transfer your money between your bank accounts, please work with your bank and utilize their ACH or wire transfer services.

 

I am not a Venmo user and I’m seeing an unauthorized charge on my bank or card statement.

If you see Venmo on your bank or card statement, please verify that no one else has authorized access to your credit card or bank account. For example, we often see family members who use their parent or spouse’s card on Venmo. For more information, visit this article.

If you still have questions regarding Venmo transactions appearing on your card or bank statements, or if you wish to dispute such transactions, please contact your bank or card company directly.

Venmo Credit Card

Cash Back to Crypto for the Venmo Credit Card FAQ

Want to use your Venmo Credit Card cash back to get into cryptocurrency? Read on to learn more. 

 

How does Cash Back to Crypto work?

When you sign up for Cash Back to Crypto for your Venmo Credit Card, we’ll use your normal cash back from your Venmo account to buy the cryptocurrency of your choosing. You can hold that crypto, buy more crypto, or sell it when you want.

 Learn more about buying, holding, and selling crypto on Venmo.

 

How do I turn on/off Cash Back to Crypto?

You can turn Cash Back to Crypto on and off in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app. Tap the toggle for Purchase crypto. At the end of your next reward cycle, your cash rewards transferred to your Venmo account will reflect how you have the toggle set.

 

Can I change which crypto I purchase for my Cash Back to Crypto?

You can update which crypto will be purchased for your Cash Back to Crypto from your Venmo account in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app:

  1. Tap Manage in the rewards tab
  2. Tap Change crypto
  3.  Pick a different cryptocurrency.

 

Will I be charged a fee for the crypto I get for my Cash Back to Crypto?

We waive the normal crypto transaction fees for Cash Back to Crypto auto-purchases made from your Venmo account. Please note that you would still incur fees for any crypto that you purchase or sell outside of the Cash Back to Crypto. The exchange rate for any crypto transaction includes a spread that Venmo earns on each purchase and sale. Learn more about the fees for buying and selling crypto on Venmo.

 

When do I get my Cash Back to Crypto?

Your cash back rewards transfer to your Venmo account 1-3 days after statement end.

If you have questions about anything related to cryptocurrency, please contact Venmo. If you have questions about your billing cycle and rewards cycle timing, please contact Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462.

 

Do I pay taxes on the crypto I get for Cash Back to Crypto?

You will need to report gains or losses from the sale of crypto on your taxes. This includes the crypto you  purchase and sell by using Cash Back to Crypto for the Venmo Credit Card. For any tax advice, you would need to speak with a tax expert. Learn more about taxes for cryptocurrency on Venmo.

 

I got cash back instead of crypto. Why?

If your rewards are ever less than a dollar at the end of your reward cycle, Venmo will provide that to you as cash back. We’ll only auto-purchase crypto for you if the cash back is greater than a dollar.

 

My reward cycle is ending and I don’t like the price of my currently selected crypto. What should I do?

You can enable and disable Cash Back to Crypto for the Venmo Credit Card in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app. You’ll get your rewards in your Venmo account in the form you’ve selected at the end of the cycle. Please note that we can’t reverse any crypto auto-purchases once they’ve been made.

View terms and conditions

Paul McCartney played bass in The Beatles.

General Venmo Credit Card Billing Questions

Want to see your Venmo Credit Card transaction history or check your billing date? Learn more here. 

 

How can I access my bill/statement? 

Electronic statements will always be sent to the email address associated with your Venmo Credit Card account. Statements are also viewable right in the Venmo app; just click the statement icon or View statements in the Venmo Credit Card section of the app to see and download a statement.

 

Where can I find my current Venmo Credit Card balance, due date, and other billing information?

Go to the Venmo Credit Card section of your Venmo app. When you tap Venmo Credit Card you will see your transaction history. You can tap on the image of your Venmo Credit Card to see your current balance, as well as your due date, remaining credit, your credit limit, and your current APR(s).  

 

I need to change my Venmo Credit Card bill’s due date

To see if you are eligible to change the due date for your Venmo Credit Card, please contact Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

A credit card billing cycle typically lasts 28 to 31 days. The number of days in each cycle can change, but it should be roughly one month.

 

How is my Minimum Payment Due and interest calculated?

Information on how the Minimum Payment Due and interest are calculated is included in the terms and rates.  

 

How do I turn on Autopay for my Venmo Credit Card?

Go to the Venmo Credit Card section of your Venmo app, then tap Venmo Credit Card, and you will see your transaction history. You can tap on the image of your Venmo Credit Card, then scroll down to the Autopay section to set up Autopay. You can set the funding source that will be used.

Please note: If you select your Venmo balance as the funding source, you will need to select a bank account as a backup funding source as well.   

If you would like to disable Autopay, you can do so by going to the same section of the Venmo app and selecting Manage Autopay. 

 

Is there a late fee for Venmo Credit Card payments? 

If you do not pay the Minimum Payment Due by your due date, you will incur a late fee**.  

 

I need to set up a payment plan to pay down my bill. How do I do that? 

If you need to set up a payment plan for your Venmo Credit Card, please contact Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. Agents are available 24 hours a day.  

** See Terms & Rates

The Venmo Credit Card is issued by Synchrony Bank pursuant to a license from Visa USA Inc. VISA is a registered trademark of Visa International Service Association and used under license.

Getting Started with your Venmo Credit Card

Excited for your new Venmo Credit Card? Learn what to do once you’re approved. 

 

I was approved! When can I start using my Venmo Credit Card? 

A virtual card number is available to you in the Venmo app as soon as you are approved for the Venmo Credit Card. That number can be used for online purchases and you can also request a new virtual number in the Venmo app (which deactivates your previous virtual card number). Please note that you will need to confirm the last 4 digits of your Social Security Number to view the virtual card number, expiration date, and CVV, but once confirmed, you’ll be able to copy this card number and shop online. 

Your Venmo Credit Card account may have a lower credit limit until you receive and activate your physical card. You can view your credit limit in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app.

Your Venmo Credit Card account is also automatically added to your Venmo payment methods and can be used to pay for things like you already do today, such as person-to-person payments and purchases with authorized merchants. Similar to other credit cards, the Venmo Credit Card is subject to Venmo’s standard 3% fee when used to make person-to-person payments.     

Once you get your physical Venmo Credit Card, you can find your account number on the back of the card.  

 

How do I activate my Venmo Credit Card?  

To activate your Venmo Credit Card, simply scan the QR code on the front of the card using your phone’s camera or the QR code scanner in the Venmo app. Alternatively, you can enter your three-digit CVV code within the Venmo app to activate.

If you would rather activate by phone, call Synchrony Bank at 855-890-6779.

 

Can I add a joint or authorized user? 

The Venmo Credit Card does not support adding joint or authorized users at this time. 

The Venmo Credit Card is issued by Synchrony Bank pursuant to a license from Visa USA Inc. VISA is a registered trademark of Visa International Service Association and used under license.

Getting the Venmo Credit Card

How do I apply for the Venmo Credit Card? 

The Venmo Credit Card is now available for eligible users. If you are eligible to apply, you will see a Venmo Credit Card section in the Cards tab of the Venmo app or when you log in at venmo.com. Learn more about the Venmo Credit Card.

What information do I need to apply for the Venmo Credit Card? 

When you apply for the Venmo Credit Card, we’ll ask you for your name, email address, phone number, and residential address. We’ll also need to gather some identity information, like your date of birth and the last four digits of your Social Security Number. In addition, we ask for your annual net income as a part of this process.  

 

Do you run a credit check as a part of this application?

When you apply and complete the Venmo Credit Card application, a soft credit card inquiry will be performed that will not impact your credit score. If you are approved, however, a hard inquiry will be performed, which may impact your credit score.

 

Should I check to see if there’s a freeze on my credit report before I apply for the Venmo Credit Card? 

If you currently have a freeze on your credit report, you will need to remove the freeze before applying for the card. If you have additional questions about this credit check, please contact Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462.  

  

What is the APR? What will my credit line be? 

Your APR(s) and credit limit(s) will be determined when you open your account, based on your creditworthiness and other factors. Learn about the terms and rates.

 

Do I need a Venmo account to apply for a Venmo Credit Card?

To apply for, use, and manage your Venmo Credit Card, you need a Venmo account that has been open for at least 30 days and is in good standing. Please note that you cannot close a Venmo account unless you have first closed your Venmo Credit Card and have paid off its balance in full. 

 

Does it cost money to apply for the Venmo Credit Card?

To review rates, fees, and terms for the Venmo Credit Card, please visithttps://venmo.com/creditcard/termsandrates.

 

Can I apply jointly with my spouse/partner?

At this time, you are unable to apply for the Venmo Credit Card with joint or authorized users. The Venmo Credit Card is not meant for multiple users.  

 

Can I have more than one Venmo Credit Card at a time?

At this time, Venmo users can only have one Venmo Credit Card.  

 

I applied for the Venmo Credit Card. What happens next?

If your application is approved, you will be provided some information about your credit limit(s) and APR(s). Please note: You may be given a temporary limit until your Venmo Credit Card is activated. Once your Venmo Credit Card account is opened, you can expect your card in 7-10 business days. 

Learn more about activating your Venmo Credit Card. If you have additional questions, please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

If your application is not approved right away, we may need to gather more information from you in order to process your application. If this happens, you’ll be notified to submit additional information in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app.

If you are not approved for a Venmo Credit Card, you’ll receive a letter in the mail explaining why in 7-10 business days after your application date. If you have further questions, please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

 

I was approved for the Venmo Credit Card. When will it get here?

You can expect your Venmo Credit Card in 7-10 business days. In the meantime, you can use your virtual card number to shop online. Learn more about using your virtual card number here. If your Venmo Credit Card doesn’t arrive in the expected window, please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

  

** See Terms & Rates

The Venmo Credit Card is issued by Synchrony Bank pursuant to a license from Visa USA Inc. VISA is a registered trademark of Visa International Service Association and used under license.

Making a Payment to Your Venmo Credit Card Bill

Need to pay your Venmo Credit Card bill? Learn what to do here. 

 

How can I make a payment toward my Venmo Credit Card bill? 

The easiest way to pay your Venmo Credit Card bill is to make the payment from your Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app
  2. Click View and Pay

From there, you can pay your bill using the money in your Venmo account or with a verified bank account on file. You can easily automate your Venmo Credit Card repayments by setting up Autopay in the Venmo app. 

A credit card billing cycle typically lasts 28 to 31 days. The number of days in each cycle can change, but it should be roughly one month.

 

Can I use money in my Venmo account to pay my Venmo Credit Card bill?

You can use the money in your Venmo account to make payments towards your Venmo Credit Card bill. However, it’s important to keep in mind that Venmo Credit Card payments are funded by a single payment method.

If you don’t have enough money in your Venmo account, but want to use the money in your Venmo account to make a payment, you can make two separate payments toward the card. First, make a one-time payment for the exact amount of money in your Venmo account, and then make another one-time payment for the remainder using a bank account. 

 

Are there other ways I can pay my Venmo Credit Card bill?  

If you would like to pay by phone, call Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462.  

If you would like to pay by check, you can mail the check along with your account number to: 

Venmo Credit Card P.O. Box 71718 Philadelphia, PA 19176-1718

Make checks payable to “Venmo Credit Card”. 

 

Where can I find my payment history? 

You can view your payment history in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app. Click on the Transactions tab to see completed and scheduled payments. You can also find a history of your payments within your Venmo Credit Card statement. 

 

How do I split my Venmo Credit Card transactions? 

You can split Venmo Credit Card transactions by sending a request to another Venmo user from the transaction detail screen. Once the request is accepted, the funds will automatically go toward your Venmo Credit Card bill.  

 

What happens if my friend doesn’t accept my Venmo Credit Card transaction request in time for my bill? 

If the person you requested to pay part or all of your bill does not complete your charge request by your payment due date, you are still responsible for paying your Venmo Credit Card bill. Venmo does not automatically charge anyone upon a charge request; it is up to the requestee to accept the charge. If you do not pay the Minimum Payment Due by your due date, you will incur a late fee**. 

*See Venmo Credit Card Rewards Program Terms. Use of cash back is subject to the terms of the Venmo User Agreement

** See Terms & Rates

The Venmo Credit Card is issued by Synchrony Bank pursuant to a license from Visa USA Inc. VISA is a registered trademark of Visa International Service Association and used under license.

Updating your Venmo Credit Card

For all the info about replacing and updating information about your Venmo Credit Card, read on here.

If you’re concerned about unauthorized activity on your Venmo Credit Card, please learn more about freezing your Venmo Credit Card and reporting any unauthorized activity.

 

I need to replace my physical Venmo Credit Card

If you need to replace a lost, stolen, or damaged Venmo Credit Card, you can do so in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Venmo Credit Card section of the Cards tab
  2. Tap Replace lost, stolen, or damaged card 

The app will walk you through reporting any unrecognized transactions, confirming your address, and will get you in contact with our partners at Synchrony Bank, if needed.

If you’d rather speak with someone over the phone about your lost, stolen, or damaged card, our partners at Synchrony Bank are able to assist. To reach them, please call the support number on the back of your Venmo Credit Card (855-878-6462). They are available by phone 24 hours a day, seven days a week.

 

I want to get a new virtual card number for my Venmo Credit Card

For peace of mind, if your virtual card number is compromised, or if you’d like a new one, you can request a new virtual card number in the Venmo Credit Card section of your Venmo app. You are limited to one request per every 24 hours. Requesting a new virtual card number will deactivate your previous virtual card number. It will not affect your physical card number. 

 

I want a new PIN for my Venmo Credit Card

If you forgot or haven’t set your PIN for your Venmo Credit Card, you can begin that process by reaching out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462.  

If you know your PIN, you can change it at any time by calling 855-820-5806. You need to know your current PIN in order to change your PIN via this phone number. 

  

I need to update the billing information on my Venmo Credit Card

If you need to update the billing information for your Venmo Credit Card, please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

 

I want to request a credit line increase

You can request a credit line increase for your Venmo Credit Card by contacting Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. Some cardholders also have the ability to request a credit line increase in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app.

Requesting a credit line increase will not result in a hard inquiry on your credit. Your request may be accepted, declined, or you may be given a lower credit line increase than requested. If your credit line request is denied or if you were given a lower amount than requested, you will receive a physical or electronic letter from Synchrony Bank explaining the decision within 7-10 business days.

 

I need to access the customer deceased notification form

To assist during this difficult time, Synchrony offers an online form to notify the Bank of the passing of a cardholder. The account record will be updated upon receipt of your submission. You can view and submit this form on Synchrony’s website.

If you need assistance with the cardholder’s Venmo account, please contact our Support team.

Using your Venmo Credit Card

Want to learn more about using your Venmo Credit Card? Look here for some frequently asked questions.

 

Where can I use the Venmo Credit Card? 

You can use your Venmo Credit Card everywhere Visa® credit cards are accepted, including within the Venmo app. The card is automatically added as a payment method in your Venmo app for payments to friends and authorized merchants.

Similar to other credit cards, the Venmo Credit Card is subject to Venmo’s standard 3% fee when used to make person-to-person payments. Person-to-person payments do not incur a cash advance fee.    

 

Can I use my Venmo Credit Card internationally?

The Venmo Credit Card can be used for international transactions. There are no foreign transaction fees.

Learn more about rates, fees and terms for the Venmo Credit Card.

 

Can I use my Venmo Credit Card to get a cash advance? 

Yes.

Learn more about cash advance fees in the terms and rates.

 

Can I make contactless payments using my Venmo Credit Card?

The Venmo Credit Card supports contactless payments. Look for the contactless symbol anywhere Visa credit cards are accepted. When prompted for payment, simply tap your Venmo Credit Card on the payment reader to make your purchase.   

 

What’s the difference between my physical card number and my virtual card number? 

There are several ways to use your Venmo Credit Card account: you can make online purchases using your virtual card number, you can use your physical card in stores and online, or you can use your Venmo Credit Card as a payment method within the Venmo app.    

Your physical card number can be used in-person and online. It comes with a chip and supports contactless payments and cash advances.   

Your virtual card number can be accessed in the Venmo Credit Card section of the Venmo app. Please note that you will need to confirm the last 4 digits of your Social Security Number to view the virtual card number, expiration date, and CVV.  

 

Can I use my Venmo Credit Card to make payments to other Venmo users?

Your Venmo Credit Card is automatically added as a payment method on Venmo. That means you can use your card to make payments to friends and authorized merchants using the Venmo app.

 

What is an authorization?

When you make a purchase using your Venmo Credit Card, the merchant will authorize your card for the initial amount of your purchase. This is called a merchant authorization, and it allows the merchant to first ensure that your Venmo Credit Card is open and working. The transaction will appear as a pending charge on your transaction history until the merchant completes the transaction and posts the final amount. 

  

What is a capture?

Funds will not be transferred to the merchant until they finalize the transaction and capture the final amount. Once a merchant captures an authorization, you’ll see the purchase finalized in your Venmo app for the final amount. Please note that most merchants will capture a transaction in 1-7 business days. If your transaction is still pending after 7 business days, please contact Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. 

  

Why was the final amount (capture) different than the initial authorization?

There may be a difference between the amount that you see when you initially authorize a purchase, and the final capture amount. Some merchants place a hold larger than the transaction amount to cover incidentals or tips (such as “pay at the pump” for fuel, or at restaurants, hotels, and car rental locations). If no incidentals took place, or you tipped more or less than the hold amount, your final transaction amount will reflect that difference.  

 

Can I add my Venmo Credit Card toanother virtual wallet?

You can add your Venmo Credit Card to virtual wallets like Samsung Pay, Apple Pay, and Google Pay™. If you have any questions about adding your card, please reach out to the specific virtual wallet provider.   

 

What are the benefits and rewards for the Venmo Credit Card?  

With the Venmo Credit Card, you can easily split purchases with friends on Venmo in the Venmo app, earn cash back rewards on your eligible purchases*, and pay your bill using the money in your Venmo account. Better yet, there’s no annual fee** for the Venmo Credit Card.  

 

What do I do if I have unauthorized activity on my Venmo Credit Card?

If you need to report any unauthorized transactions, please reach out to Synchrony Bank at 855-878-6462. You will not be liable for unauthorized use on your account, but you will be responsible for all use by anyone you allow to use your account. If you would like to temporarily disable your physical card for any reason, you can do so in the Venmo Credit Card section of your Venmo app. You can return to the same section when you are ready to re-enable your physical card. Learn more about temporarily disabling your Venmo Credit Card. 

The Venmo Credit Card is issued by Synchrony Bank pursuant to a license from Visa USA Inc. VISA is a registered trademark of Visa International Service Association and used under license.

Venmo Credit Card Referrals FAQ + Terms 

How does Refer a Friend work? 

If you are a Venmo Visa® Credit Cardholder with a Venmo account in good standing, you can earn a $50 referral reward in your Venmo account through the referral program if the following criteria are met: 

  • You find the Refer a Friend offer presented to you in the Venmo app or other communication channel
  • You send the referral link included in the offer to your Venmo friend(s)
  • Your Venmo friend, who must have a Venmo account open for at least 30 days and in good standing, uses your referral link from a mobile device to apply for the Venmo Credit Card in the Venmo app (app version 9.7 or greater) within fourteen (14) calendar days of the link creation
  • Your Venmo friend is subsequently approved for the Venmo Credit Card 

How many referral rewards can I get by inviting my friends? 

As a referrer, you can earn up to (10) $50 referral rewards (for a maximum $500) when your Venmo friends meet the criteria already mentioned. Additional terms apply. More details below.

 

How fast do I get my referral reward after my Venmo friend is approved for the Venmo Credit Card? 

If your Venmo friend uses your referral link to apply for the Venmo Credit Card from a mobile device in the Venmo app (app version 9.7 or greater) within 14 days of the creation of the referral link and is subsequently approved for the card, you will be sent a notification about your referral reward and receive it (if you haven’t already received the maximum) within approximately 24 hours, but it may take up to 2 weeks. Due to the confidential nature of the credit approval process, we will not disclose any information to you about who has applied.

 

How can I share my referral link with a Venmo friend? 

You can invite your Venmo friends to apply for the Venmo Credit Card from a mobile device in the Venmo app (app version 9.7 or greater) by tapping the Share your link button on the Venmo Credit Card referral offer screen in the Venmo app or as otherwise communicated to you.  

From there, you can choose to share your referral link by text message, email, social media, or have the option to manually paste your referral link into a different communication channel of your choice. You may only share your link with people you know, and may not publicly post it in group forums that are likely to include people you don’t personally know as stated in the Venmo User Agreement. 

 

TERMS

By participating in the Venmo Credit Card referral promotion (the “Program”), you agree to the following terms and conditions (“Terms”). Venmo and Synchrony may at any time and in its sole discretion, without prior notice, terminate, cancel, suspend or modify the Program or these terms. Under these terms, if you are the person inviting friends to apply, you are a “referrer” and the Venmo friend who receives your referral link is a “referee”.

Eligibility and Overview 

  1. You may participate in this Program as a referrer until the Program is terminated or modified by Venmo or Synchrony. 

 

  1. To be eligible to earn referral rewards through the Program, referrer must be 18 years old (or age of majority in their state of residence) and have a Venmo account in good standing for at least 30 days (“Valid Venmo Account”) and a Venmo Credit Card account in good standing at the time of participation through when the referral reward(s) are issued. In addition, each referee must apply for a new Venmo Credit Card account by tapping on the referral link from their mobile device and submit their application using the Venmo app (app version 9.7 or greater), and subsequently be approved for the card, in order for referrer to be eligible to earn a referral reward.

  1. Each referee must complete all the following steps (“Reward Criteria”): 
    • Apply for the Venmo Credit Card in the Venmo app (app version 9.7 or greater) using their mobile device and the referral link provided by the referrer within 14 calendar days of the link creation. 
    • Subsequently be approved for a Venmo Credit Card.

Limits on Referrals: Referrers may only refer family and personal friends and acquaintances. Additionally, you must not publish or distribute the Invitation, the referral link, or any notifications received from Venmo containing the referral link online or otherwise where the audience is likely to include people who are not your family or personal friends and acquaintances. If you do so, Venmo may disqualify you from participation in the Program and any successful referrals will be void, in Venmo’s sole discretion. There is a limit of ten (10) referral rewards per referrer Venmo Credit Card Account.  If two or more referrers invite the same referee, Venmo will credit the referral reward to the person who has their Invitation accepted first, as determined in Venmo’s sole discretion.

Referral Reward: The referral reward is $50 USD, sent to the Valid Venmo Account that qualified for the referral reward for each successful referral. Referral rewards should post to your Valid Venmo Account within approximately twenty-four (24) hours but may take up to two weeks.  

Receiving and Using Referral Rewards: 

  • Venmo reserves the right to limit, cancel, delay, or revoke a referral reward if Venmo determines, in its sole discretion, that a referrer or referee has violated these Terms or the Venmo User Agreement or has acted in a fraudulent or abusive manner, or if Venmo restricts or closes their Valid Venmo Account pursuant to the Venmo User Agreement and/or the Valid Venmo Credit Card is restricted or closed pursuant to the Venmo Credit Card Account Agreement.  
  • Venmo may provide an alternate referral reward of equal value if it is unable for any reason to fulfill the referral reward. Referral reward redemptions are final, will not be returned, and are subject to review and verification.

Venmo Debit Card

Add Cash in Stores

How do I add money to my Venmo Debit Card in stores? 

You can add cash to your Venmo account in-store using your Venmo Debit Card. 

Start by locating a Walmart store near you and head to any staffed register (note: this service is not currently available in Vermont). Tell the clerk you are looking to add money to your Venmo Debit Card. 

A couple of things to keep in mind: 

  • The processor charges a $3.74 fee per transaction for adding cash via this method 
  • You will need to be able to swipe your Venmo Debit Card physically 
  • If you’re depositing more than $300, you will be asked to provide an ID that matches the name on your Venmo Debit Card.   

Once you make the deposit, you’ll see the transaction in the Venmo app within minutes. 

Keep in mind, Venmo does have other forms of adding money that do not contain any fees. Learn more about adding money to Venmo account. 

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

What are the limits for adding money to my Venmo Debit Card in stores? 

There are a few limits for adding money to your Venmo Debit Card in stores:

  • $20-$500 per transaction
  • Maximum of 3 transactions for a combined total of $999.99 per rolling 24 hours
  • Maximum of 7 transactions for a combined total of $3,000 per rolling 7 days
  • Maximum of 20 transactions for a combined total of $4,000 per calendar month

If the transaction doesn’t appear in your app, check in with the cashier or money center for a faster resolution. If you are still having issues, please contact us. Learn more about adding money to your Venmo account.

Authorizations for Venmo Debit Card

What is an authorization?

When you make a purchase using your Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card, the merchant will place a hold on the funds in your available Venmo balance to cover the initial amount of your purchase. This is called a merchant authorization, and it allows the merchant to ensure that your Venmo account is open and working.

The authorization ensures that you have sufficient funds in your Venmo balance to cover the transaction amount. The transaction will appear as a pending charge in your personal transactions feed until the merchant completes (captures) the transaction and posts the final amount. 

 

What is a capture?

Funds will not be transferred to the merchant until they finalize the transaction and capture the final amount. Once a merchant captures an authorization, you’ll see the purchase completed for the final amount in your personal transactions feed.

Please note, that while most merchants may capture a transaction in 1-5 days, the merchant may take up to 30 days to finalize the transaction.

  

Why was the final amount (capture) different than the initial authorization?

This is because some merchants place a hold larger than the transaction amount to cover incidentals or tips (such as “pay at the pump” for fuel, or at restaurants, hotels, and car rental locations), which will impact your available balance while the authorization is pending. If no incidentals took place, or you tipped less than the hold amount, your final transaction amount will reflect that difference.

Please see the Venmo Mastercard Cardholder Agreement for further details. 

  

Why is my balance less than expected before the payment is finalized?

Right after you make a purchase (authorization), all or part of your Venmo balance will be held until the purchase is finalized (captured) by the merchant. If your Venmo balance exceeds the amount of your purchase, your balance will be reduced by the authorized amount to account for this hold. This is to ensure that there are enough funds to cover the purchase once it is finalized.   

However, if the amount of your purchase exceeds your available Venmo balance, the transaction will be declined.

 If the initial authorization was for a greater amount than the final capture, any funds held in excess of the final purchase amount will be made available within 30 days of completion of the transaction.    

 

How long will my transaction remain pending?

Different kinds of transactions can remain pending for different amounts of time. While most transactions may complete within 7 days, authorizations from businesses like hotels and rental car companies may remain for a few days longer. Merchants have up to 30 days to finalize the transaction. Please see the Venmo Mastercard Cardholder Agreement for further details. 

In these scenarios, Venmo needs to allow the merchant enough time to complete or void the transaction before we can intervene. If you suspect that a transaction has been pending for too long, please contact us here. 

 

Why did this merchant charge me again when I already paid for my order?

It’s not uncommon for online merchants to authorize your Venmo Debit Card for the full amount of your order, then individually capture the amounts for any parts of your order shipping separately. This means that you may see a pending transaction for your expected total, followed by smaller transactions that make up the different parts of that total.

Once the merchant has shipped all of your items, they’ll typically capture the smaller transactions and release any funds held for the total transaction amount back to your Venmo balance. 

 

How do I cancel a recurring Venmo Debit Card payment or subscription?

Please contact the merchant directly to cancel any recurring payments or preauthorized transfers.  

If the merchant is unable to help, please contact Venmo Support for assistance.

Contactless Venmo Debit Card Payments

Want to make quick, easy payments with your Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card? Check out contactless payments. 

 

How do I make contactless payments using my Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card?

Look for the contactless symbol at checkout everywhere Mastercard® is accepted. When prompted for payment, simply tap your Venmo Debit Card on the payment reader to make your purchase. Make sure you are within an inch or two of the payment reader to ensure it can read your card.

 

Where can I make a contactless payment?

Everywhere Mastercard is accepted and you see the contactless symbol  at checkout, you can tap to pay. Use this search to find participating locations near you!

 

When I tap my card it doesn’t work. What should I do?

You can still use your card if a contactless payment does not work. You just need to swipe or insert your Venmo Debit Card into the payment reader.

If you run into this problem once, the error could be with the payment terminal. If contactless payments consistently don’t work for you, please email us at debitcardsupport@venmo.com so we help.

 

I would like to use Venmo QR codes in stores

Venmo QR codes are an easy way to use your Venmo account at checkout with certain in-store merchants. Learn more about using Venmo QR codes in stores.

Customer Identification for the Venmo Debit Card

Why do I have to provide Venmo with my SSN and other personal information?

IMPORTANT INFORMATION ABOUT PROCEDURES FOR OPENING A NEW CARD ACCOUNT: To help the government fight the funding of terrorism and money laundering activities, federal law requires all financial institutions to obtain, verify, and record information that identifies each person who opens a Card Account. 

What this means for you: When you open a Card Account, we will ask for your name, address, date of birth, and other information that will allow us to identify you. We may also ask to see a copy of your driver’s license or other identifying documents. 

When you apply for the card make sure to use your home address and not work address or P.O. Box so that we are able to verify your identity. 

For more information regarding money transfer business regulations, please click here. 

  

Which address should I use for my application?

You should use your home address. At this time we cannot accept P.O. Box addresses. 

  

Which documents can I provide if I need to submit additional information?

There are different documentation requirements based on the additional information we need to confirm. You can see what additional documents we are requesting by navigating to Settings → Venmo Debit Card in the Venmo app. From there, you can upload your documents directly for review.

See below for a list of acceptable documents.

The following documents can be used if we need more info about your name and date of birth: 

  • U.S. passport 
  • Driver license 
  • U.S. government-issued ID 
  • ITIN Assignment letter 
  • DHS card 
  • Tribal ID card 

Please make sure your picture of the document is clear and legible.   

The following documents can be used if we need more info about your proof of address: 

  • Bank, credit card, or 401K/brokerage statement (from the past 12 months) 
  • Utility bill (gas, electric, cable, internet, or phone from the past 12 months) 
  • W2 or paystub (from the past 12 months) 
  • Lease for residence (dated in the past 12 months) 
  • Current car registration (dated in the past 12 months) 
  • IRS letter (dated in the past 12 months). We cannot accept a tax return for this documentation
  • You can also submit the following documents if they were issued to you within the last 12 months: 
    • Unexpired driver’s license (with current address) 
    • Unexpired U.S. government-issued ID (with current address) 

Please make sure your picture of the document is clear and legible.   

The following documents can be used if we need more info about your Social Security Number: 

  • IRS letter (dated in the past 12 months) 
  • SSN card 
  • ITIN Assignment letter 

Please make sure your picture of the document is clear and legible. 

  

How long will it take to review the documents I submitted?

We review documents as quickly as we can, typically within 5 business days. You’ll receive an update once we complete our review or if we run into any issues verifying your info. We appreciate your patience. 

 

I already provided my personal information to you once. Do I need to provide it again to get a Venmo Debit Card?

If you have already verified your identity in the Venmo app, you won’t need to do so a second time. When you apply for the Venmo Debit Card, you’ll be asked to confirm the name and address you provided when you first verified your identity.

The Venmo Debit Card may be used everywhere Mastercard is accepted in the U.S. Mastercard is a registered trademark, and the circles design is a trademark of Mastercard International Incorporated.

Expiring Venmo Debit Cards

Is your Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card’s expiration date coming soon? Learn how to get a new one. 

 

I received a notification that my Venmo Debit Card is expiring soon

You’ll be notified that it’s time to request a replacement Venmo Debit Card within 60 days of your current card’s expiration date. For example, if your card expires at the end of December, you’ll be prompted to order a replacement in November. Cards expire on the last day of the expiration month.

You’ll then be able to request your new card by tapping the in-app notification or going to the Venmo Debit Card section of the Cards tab in the app.

You may be prompted to complete a transaction if you haven’t used your old card. You can also update your shipping address by tapping the pencil icon next to your address. 

 

My new card arrived - now what?

It’s important to activate your new card right away once it arrives. Replacement cards that are not activated within 90 days will be permanently closed. Once your new card is activated, the expired card should be destroyed.

Fees for the Venmo Debit Card

Getting started with your Venmo Mastercard®  Debit Card and want to know what fees are involved? Learn more here. 

 

Does it cost money to get the card?

There isn’t a fee to apply for a Venmo Mastercard®  Debit Card.

Learn more about fees on Venmo.

 

Are there fees for purchases using the Venmo Debit Card?

There are no fees when you use your Venmo Debit Card for purchases.

 

Are there fees for using my Venmo Debit Card to get cash?

There may be fees associated with obtaining cash with your Venmo Debit Card if you use an ATM or Over the Counter Withdrawal.

You may withdraw funds from your Venmo balance at an ATM that displays the Mastercard®, PULSE® or Cirrus® acceptance marks. A $2.50 ATM Domestic Withdrawal Fee applies. You may also complete a withdrawal at a bank or financial institution. A $3.00 Over the Counter Withdrawal fee applies when a signature is required at the bank or financial institution. Additional ATM owner or bank fees may apply.

If you are looking for ways to withdraw cash using your Venmo Debit Card at no cost, there are options! You can withdraw funds from your balance at no cost when using a MoneyPass® ATM within the U.S. Find MoneyPass ATMs.

Cashback with a purchase is also available at Point of Sale devices at no-cost, as permissible by a merchant that bears the Maestro® or PULSE acceptance marks, and is dependent on retailer participation. Please verify acceptance with retailer at the time of the transaction.

Please note that ATM withdrawals, Over the Counter Withdrawals, and cashback with a purchase at Point of Sale devices are Venmo balance funded only. 

See the Venmo Mastercard Debit Cardholder Agreement for full details

My Venmo Debit Card is lost, stolen, or damaged

If your Venmo Mastercard®  Debit Card has been lost, stolen, or damaged, this article can help.

 

How can I reorder my lost or stolen Venmo Debit Card?

You can order a replacement debit card by heading to the Cards tab and tapping on your Venmo Debit Card.

Here’s how to reorder your Venmo Debit Card:

  1. Tap Replace lost, stolen, or damaged card
  2. Select Lost or Stolen
  3. If you would like to choose a new color for your debit card, select the pencil icon
  4. Review your transactions:
    • If there are no suspicious transactions, select These transactions are ok
    • If there are suspicious transactions, select Something’s wrong, get support and give us a call
  5. Review your info - to make changes tap the pencil icon, or tap Confirm to continue
  6. Select Done

 

How can I reorder my damaged Venmo Debit Card?

You can order a replacement debit card by heading to the Cards tab and tapping on your Venmo Debit Card.

Here’s how to reorder your Venmo Debit Card:

  1. Tap Replace lost, stolen, or damaged card
  2. Select Damaged
  3. If you would like to choose a new color for your debit card, select the pencil icon
  4. Review your info - to make changes tap the pencil icon, or tap Confirm to continue
  5. Select Done

Once you complete this process, you will see a confirmation screen advising you that a new Venmo Debit Card is on its way. 

 

Why am I not seeing the option to order a replacement Venmo Debit Card?

If you don’t see the option to order a replacement card, we need to confirm additional information before we send you a new card.

Please contact us and we’ll assist you in getting a new card.

 

I’m contacting Venmo Support for a new Venmo Debit Card, but my address has changed. Can it be shipped somewhere else?

We can update your shipping address, but we need to verify it first. You can submit one of the following documents to our Support team:  

  • Unexpired driver license 
  • Unexpired U.S. government issued ID 
  • Bank, credit card, or 401K/brokerage statement (from the past 12 months) 
  • Utility bill (gas, electric, cable, internet, or phone from the past 12 months) 
  • W2 or paystub (from the past 12 months) 
  • Lease for residence (dated in the past 12 months) 
  • Current car registration (dated in the past 12 months) 
  • IRS letter (dated in the past 12 months) 

Please make sure your picture of the document is clear and legible.

Please note: We are not able to ship a Venmo Debit Card to a P.O. box.

Splitting and Sharing Venmo Debit Card Transactions

After you make a purchase with your Venmo Mastercard Debit Card, you will be prompted with the option to split or share your purchase. You can also split or share purchases from your personal transactions feed in the Venmo app.

 

How can I split my Venmo Debit Card Transactions?

You can send a charge request and customize the amount to split a purchase with one or many users. You’ll be able to see the total amount requested as you change the charge amount for each user, whether the requested total is above or below the split amount. (Any split transaction will not affect your original payment amount.)

You also have the option of excluding yourself from the split calculation. When you split a purchase, you’ll send charge requests to other users. The other users must accept the request in order to complete the payment.

 

Can I cancel a split request?

If you need to cancel a pending payment request from a split, go to the Me tab and find the transaction at the top of your Transactions feed. From here, you can cancel a split request or remind the other user(s) about the request.

 

Are my purchases private?

As with payments on Venmo, you can set the privacy level for each individual purchase. All purchases default to the Private privacy setting so you can choose which you want to share and when.

You can share a purchase (which will change the privacy settings on the purchase) by tapping on it from your personal transactions feed under the Me tab and selecting Share. If you do choose to share your activity, we will always keep the purchase amount private.

The Venmo Debit Card may be used everywhere Mastercard is accepted in the U.S. Mastercard is a registered trademark, and the circles design is a trademark of Mastercard International Incorporated

Using Your Venmo Debit Card

How can I update my billing address for my Venmo Debit Card?

You can update this information in the Venmo Debit Card section of your app.

Follow these instructions to update your address for your Venmo Debit card:

  1. Go to the Cards tab 
  2. Check under the Venmo Debit Card section
  3. Tap the image of your card
  4. Tap Update under your listed address.

If you are updating your address due to a declined purchase, please note that updating your address does not guarantee that your purchase will be approved. If you experience additional declines, please contact us, so we can take a closer look.

 

Can I use my Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card to make ATM withdrawals? 

Yes! You can use your Venmo Debit Card to make cash withdrawals at ATMs or, as cash back when completing a purchase, nationwide. You’ll need your Venmo Debit Card PIN to obtain cash (if you have forgotten the PIN that you set during card activation, you can reset it in the Venmo app under Settings > Venmo Debit Card). You can withdraw funds from your balance at no cost when using a MoneyPass ATM within the U.S. and in some U.S. territories.

If you run into any issues with your ATM withdrawal please do not hesitate to contact us. 

Note: Look for the Mastercard®, PULSE®, Cirrus® or MoneyPass® acceptance marks for ATMs, and the Mastercard, Maestro®, or PULSE acceptance marks at the Point of Sale.

Learn more about fees for the Venmo Debit Card

Locate MoneyPass ATM’s here.

 

Can I get cash back on my Venmo Debit Card Purchase?

Cash back with a purchase is also available when making a Venmo Debit Card purchase at no-cost, as permissible by a merchant that bears the Mastercard, Maestro, or PULSE acceptance marks, and is dependent on retailer participation. Please verify acceptance with the retailer at the time of the transaction. 

Please note: Any cash withdrawal (ATM, cash back with a purchase at Point of Sale devices, or Over the Counter) is funded solely with your Venmo balance. Withdrawals are limited to the funds in your Venmo balance, up to a $1,000 USD daily withdrawal limit.

See the Venmo Mastercard Cardholder Agreement for full details. 

 

Do I need to move money from my Venmo account to my Venmo Debit Card?

The Venmo Debit Card uses the money in your Venmo balance, so there aren’t any extra steps you’ll need to take unless you want to add more money to your balance.

Learn more about adding money to your balance 

 

Where can I use my Venmo Debit Card?

Everywhere Mastercard is accepted in the U.S. and in U.S. territories.

Please note: Venmo and the Venmo Debit Card cannot be used with international merchants, even if you are placing an online order from the U.S. 

  

Can I use my Venmo Debit Card outside of the US?

The Venmo Debit Card may currently only be used in the U.S. and U.S territories, at U.S. merchants for transactions in USD. Please note Venmo and the Venmo Debit Card cannot be used with international merchants, even if you are placing an online order from the U.S. 

 

Can I see my Venmo Debit Card number in the Venmo app? 

Once you have been approved for the Venmo Debit Card, you can find your card details in the app by following the steps below.

  1. Go to the Cards tab in the Venmo app 
  2. Select your Venmo Debit Card 
  3. Next to Debit Card Details, select View

You can copy your card details and use them wherever MasterCard is accepted. To help keep your card details secure, you’ll be required to enable a passcode or biometric unlock to view your card details.

Learn more about reordering your Venmo Debit Card.

 

Where do I find my card transaction history?

Open the Venmo app, go to the “Me” tab. You can view all of your transaction history including, Venmo Debit Card purchases, ATM withdrawals and fees, online purchases, payments to friends, and more in your personal transactions feed on the Venmo App. 

You can also view your Venmo transaction history on the web. Learn more about downloading your Venmo transaction history.  

If you have questions about a specific transaction, contact us here. Please be prepared to provide us with the name and location of the merchant you paid, along with the date and amount of the transaction. 

  

Why was the final amount (capture) different than the initial authorization?

There may be a difference between the amount that you see when you initially authorize a purchase, and the final capture amount. Some merchants place a hold larger than the transaction amount to cover incidentals or tips (such as “pay at the pump” for fuel, or at restaurants, hotels, and car rental locations), which will impact your available balance while the authorization is pending. If no incidentals took place, or you tipped less than the held amount, your final transaction amount will reflect that difference.

Please see the Venmo Mastercard Cardholder Agreement for further details. 

  

Why is my balance less than expected before the payment is finalized?

Right after you make a purchase (authorization), all or part of your Venmo balance will be held until the purchase is finalized (captured) by the merchant. If your Venmo balance exceeds the amount of your purchase, your balance will be reduced by the authorized amount to account for this hold. This is to ensure that there are enough funds to cover the purchase once it is finalized. 

However, if the amount of your purchase exceeds your available Venmo balance, the transaction may be declined. 

If the initial authorization was for a greater amount than the final capture, any funds held in excess of the final purchase amount will be made available within 30 days of completion of the transaction. 

Learn more about Venmo Debit Card authorizations. 

 

I used my Venmo Debit Card at a gas station and now I have a pending charge that’s much larger than I expected!

When you purchase fuel at the pump, your card will be authorized when you first swipe it for an amount that’s higher than the actual amount of gas you will likely purchase. This happens to ensure that you have funds available for the purchase. While the exact amount varies by gas station, the merchant often authorizes your payment method up to $100. 

Once the final purchase amount (capture) is received, the authorization will be removed and any remaining held funds will be released. Venmo has to wait to give the merchant time to complete the transaction on their end, but some merchants may capture and finalize the sale in under 3 days. 

One way to avoid this large authorization is to pay the cashier for a specific amount of gas.  

 

Why am I getting the message “Something went wrong?” when trying to view my card details? 

This may happen due to a security alert. Please try to access the information again later. We are unable to override this security measure or provide your card details if you contact us.

Venmo Debit Card Activation

How do I activate my Venmo Mastercard Debit Card? 

When you’ve received your physical Venmo Debit Card and are ready to activate it, follow these steps in your Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Cards tab
  2. Select the Venmo Debit Card section 
  3. Tap on the Activate 
  4. Enter the CVV listed on the card
  5. Create a PIN for your card (or enter the PIN if you’ve already set one up)

Once you see the green notification indicator, your card is ready for use!

 

Can I order more than one Venmo Debit Card? Can I add an additional card for someone else to my Venmo account?  

The Venmo Debit Card links to your Venmo account. You can only have 1 active Venmo Debit Card associated with your Venmo account, and it must be in the name associated with the account.

Venmo Debit Card Refunds and Credits

Expecting some money back to your Venmo Mastercard® Debit Card? Check out this article to learn what to anticipate. 

 

Why is my refund/incoming transfer pending? 

In order to provide the merchant with time to review their transaction, you may see your money pending before it is available for use.

If your credit is unavailable to you within 30 minutes, please contact us.

 

Can you release the pending refund/incoming transfer into my account? 

If your refund has been pending for longer than 30 minutes, please contact us and we will be happy to take a closer look at it for you.

 

Why did my refund/incoming transfer get deposited and then reversed? 

Generally, this means the merchant has canceled or reversed this transaction. In your transaction history, you will see a deposit and then a withdrawal from the same merchant.

If this is incorrect or you are looking for more information, we recommend reaching out to the merchant you were expecting the funds from.

Venmo Debit Card Shipping & Arrival Time

How long will it take to get my Venmo Debit Card? I haven’t received my card yet

Once approved, your Venmo Debit Card should arrive within 10 business days. The card can only be sent to a home address at this time.

If 10 business days have passed and you have still not received your card don’t hesitate to contact us.

Keep in mind that business days exclude weekends as well as holidays observed by USPS.

Venmo Offers

What is Venmo Offers?

Venmo Offers is a program for Venmo users that offers cashback on purchases with select merchants. If you have a Venmo Mastercard Debit Card, you’re enrolled in Venmo Offers. Otherwise, you can enroll in Venmo Offers in the Offers section of your Settings menu in the Venmo app.

Offers are automatically applied to your account. You can find current Venmo offers in the Offers section of the Venmo app (accessible via the Cards tab or your Settings menu).

 

How do I earn cashback?

You can earn cashback by making eligible purchases at select merchants. Current merchant offers are shown in the Offers section of the Venmo app. A pending purchase will show estimated cashback in your feed, but the amount is not disbursed to your Venmo account until the charge is completed by the merchant. Once the transaction is complete, any earned cashback will be applied to your Venmo account. 

 

Can I earn cashback on online purchases?

Many offers include online purchases. Because each offer is different, be sure to view the offer details to see if a specific offer includes cashback on online purchases. You can find offer details in the Offers section of the Venmo app.

 

When will I receive my cashback?

A pending purchase will show your estimated cashback earned in your feed, but the amount is not disbursed to your Venmo account until the charge is completed by the merchant. Once the transaction is completed, any earned cashback will be automatically applied to your Venmo account.

Note: Transactions typically complete within 1-5 days, but can take up to 30 days depending on the merchant.

 

Do Venmo Offers expire?

Offers are only available when they are present in the Offers section of your Venmo app. It’s best to check your Venmo app to make sure a specific offer is still available before making your purchase. The cashback that you receive from completed, eligible transactions will never expire - these funds are placed in your Venmo account.

 

Are there any limits to the amount of cashback I can receive?

Certain offers have limits. Each offer is different, so it’s important to check the terms for each offer. For more information on a specific offer, you can check the Offers section of the app and select the offer you want to learn more about.

 

Who can see my cashback?

The cashback payments you receive will have the same privacy setting as your payments to other Venmo users.

  • If your payments are set to “Public”, then your cashback payments will be visible to your friends and contacts via the feed. They may also appear in the Public Feed that constantly updates to show Venmo users’ transactions in real-time and in other places on the internet. 
  • If your payments are set to “Private”, then your cashback payments will not be visible to other Venmo users. You can learn more about adjusting your privacy setting here. 

 

What is Dosh?

Venmo Offers is powered by Dosh. When you make a transaction with your Venmo Debit Card or Venmo account, Dosh checks to see if it is eligible for a cashback reward. If the transaction is eligible for a reward, Dosh will send cashback to your Venmo account after the merchant completes the charge. 

 

What information does Venmo share with Dosh?

Venmo and Dosh respect your privacy and take precautions to ensure the security of your data. Venmo only shares the data necessary to enable Dosh to provide the optimal user experience and facilitate cashback fulfillment.

For more information on Dosh’s privacy policy, view the terms of service here. You can also check out Venmo’s privacy policy here.

 

Why can’t I add my Venmo Debit Card to Dosh?

Venmo Debit Cardholders are automatically enrolled in Venmo Offers powered by Dosh. Because Venmo Debit Cardholders are already eligible for Venmo Offers, they will not be able to add any Venmo Debit Cards to Dosh’s platform.

 

Missing cashback? We can help

If you believe you are missing cashback for an offer, we can double-check it for you. 

First, make sure that the transaction in question is completed, not pending. Pending transactions will show at the top of your personal transactions feed under the “Me” tab.

If your transaction is still pending, you’ll need to wait until it is completed by the merchant before you can receive any cashback. 

If the transaction in question is completed, please contact us for help.

The Venmo Debit Card may be used everywhere Mastercard is accepted in the U.S. Mastercard is a registered trademark, and the circles design is a trademark of Mastercard International Incorporated.

Visa+

General Questions about Visa+

Want to learn more about sending or receiving money using Visa+? Check out this article.

 

What is Visa+ and what are the benefits? 

Visa+ is a way to move money between Venmo and PayPal. You can send money from your Venmo balance to a PayPal account, or receive money to your Venmo account from a PayPal account.

You can also use Visa+ to receive payments to your Venmo account from other apps.

There aren’t any fees for moving money from Venmo to PayPal (or vice versa). Transfers can take up to 30 minutes to complete.

 

Do I need to have a PayPal account to use this? 

You don’t need a PayPal account to use Visa+. As long as the person you are sending money to has an active Visa+ Payname, you can send them money from your Venmo account.

 

How much can I send/receive with Visa+? 

You are limited to $2500 per Visa+ transaction. These transactions can be declined for reasons other than the limit.

 

Are there any tax liabilities I need to be aware of by using Visa+? 

Currently, Visa+ is not meant for commercial transactions. You should talk to a financial advisor to learn more about your tax liabilities.

 

How do I deactivate my Payname? What does it mean to deactivate a Payname?

You can deactivate your Visa+ Payname in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Send & receive money with Visa+
  4. Tap the toggle to Deactivate your payname

When your Payname is deactivated, you will no longer be able to receive Visa+ payments. You will, however, be able to send Visa+ payments and send and receive other Venmo payments. You can reactivate your Visa+ Payname by following the same steps.

Receiving Money with Visa+

Are you receiving a payment from another platform to your Venmo account? Learn more about how Visa+ works.

 

Someone is sending me a Visa+ payment. How soon do I receive the money?

Visa+ payments can take up to 30 minutes to process. If you still don’t see the payment after 30 minutes have passed, contact our Support team.

 

I’m sending myself a Visa+ payment via a different app. How do I set this up?  

You can sign up for Visa+ and receive payments via supported third-party apps:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Send & receive money with Visa+
  4. The app will walk you through verifying your identity (if needed) and setting up a Payname for your Venmo account
    • Your Payname is a username that will be used to receive money to your Venmo account. It cannot be changed.
  5. Tap Agree and create to set up your Visa+ Payname

Once you’re signed up, you can use your Payname on supported apps to receive payments. You’ll also be able to tap Send to make a Visa+ payment to a PayPal account.

 

I’m sending myself a Visa+ payment via a different app. How long does it take to receive the money?

Visa+ payments can take up to 30 minutes to process. If you still don’t see the payment after 30 minutes have passed, you should contact the platform you used to send the payment.

 

Someone is trying to send me a Visa+ payment, but it won’t go through

You can’t receive Visa+ payments while your Payname is deactivated. You can reactivate your Visa+ Payname in the Venmo app:

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Send & receive money with Visa+
  4. Tap the Deactivate your payname toggle

Once your Payname is active, you can ask the sender to try the payment again. If it still doesn’t work, please ask the sender to contact the relevant Support team for assistance.

Sending Money with Visa+

Do you need to send money from Venmo to another platform? Visa+ can help. 

 

How do I send money on Visa+ ?

If you want to send money to PayPal via Visa+, the first step is confirming the recipient’s Payname. Visa+ payments cannot be cancelled or reversed, so it’s important to make sure they’re going to the right place. 

Once you’re sure you have the correct Payname, follow these steps: 

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Send & receive money with Visa+ 
  4. Tap Send
  5. Enter the recipient’s Payname
  6. Enter the dollar amount you want to send
  7. Confirm the payment details and then hit Next
    • Remember: Visa+ payments cannot be cancelled or reversed. Please ensure the recipient’s details are correct. Payments sent to the wrong Payname or account could be lost.
  8. You’ll need to confirm the last four digits of the recipient’s phone number to complete the payment

It can take up to 30 minutes for the money to get to the recipient.

 

My recipient didn’t get the money I sent

Due to the way these payments process, it can take up to 30 minutes for the recipient to see the payment. If your recipient still doesn’t see the payment after 30 minutes, or if the payment went to the wrong person, contact our Support team. 

 

How can I file a dispute?

Learn more about opening a dispute. 

 

What are the limits for a Visa+ payment? 

There’s a $2500 per transaction limit for Visa+ payments. These transactions can be declined for reasons other than the limit.

Setting Up Visa+ on Venmo

Do you need to send money from Venmo to PayPal? Look here for info about setting up Visa+

 

How do I sign up for Visa+?

You can sign up for Visa+ to send money from Venmo to a PayPal account (or vice versa):

  1. Go to the Me tab
  2. Tap the Settings gear in the top right
  3. Tap Send & receive money with Visa+
  4. The app will walk you through verifying your identity (if needed) and setting up a Payname
    • Your Payname is a username that will be used to receive money to your Venmo account. It cannot be changed.
  5. Tap Agree and create to set up your Visa+ profile

Once you’re signed up, you can share your Payname with people who want to send money to your Venmo account, or tap Send to make a payment to a PayPal account.

 

Why am I being asked to submit personal information to use Visa?

Venmo is required to gather certain information from users before they can utilize Visa+. This verification also allows you to send money to PayPal. If you’d rather not submit this information, you can still use other Venmo features. 

 

I’m having an issue with my Payname

Learn more about payname requirements.

What is a Visa+ Payname?

Want to learn more about Visa+? Start by learning more about Visa+ Paynames

 

What is a Visa+ Payname?

A Visa+ Payname is a unique handle, like a username, that you can use to send and receive money to and from people on PayPal.

Your Payname will start with a “+.” On Venmo, your Payname will include a “.Venmo” ending, like “+JohnSmith.Venmo”.

Your Payname can’t be changed after it is created.

 

Do I use the same Payname for Venmo and other platforms?

Your Payname is unique to the platform, so if you have a PayPal account, you can have a Venmo Payname and a separate PayPal Payname.

 

What are the requirements for a Payname?

There are a few requirements when creating a Visa+ Payname:

  • It must start with a letter
  • It must be between 5 and 30 characters
  • It can contain only:
    • Letters
    • Numbers
    • Hyphens (-)
    • Underscores(_)

If your preferred Payname has already been taken by another user, you’ll need to pick a different Payname.

You may not create a Payname for Venmo that is affiliated with another person or that is otherwise deceptive or misleading.
